Jobs POST /v1/scans (reserve 1 credit, return N upload slots) → PUT to signed URLs → POST /v1/scans/{id}/submit (validate the manifest: count, sizes, SHA-256) → GET /v1/scans/{id} → DELETE /v1/scans/{id} State: AWAITING_UPLOAD → QUEUED → PROCESSING → SUCCEEDED / FAILED / EXPIRED. Every read and write checks job.account_id == caller (IDOR is the #1 bug class to test).
Queue At-least-once, a lease with heartbeat, max 2 attempts, then the dead-letter queue. The worker is idempotent on job_id.
GPU worker interface ReconstructionBackend { fun reconstruct(inputs: CaptureSet, hints: ScaleHints): ReconResult }. 3d-capture.md §1 picks the first implementation: self-hosted COLMAP 4.2 with ARCore pose priors, then mvs-texturing, meshoptimizer/xatlas and glTF-Transform, on Modal serverless L4s, with Cloudflare R2 storage. 2DGS is the later path, and vendors are fallbacks behind the same interface. Metric scale: a similarity transform (Umeyama) from the reconstruction's camera centres to the ARCore poses, with residuals reported. Output contract (aligned with research §2.1): GLB with one JPEG base-colour atlas ≤ 2048², ≤ 50k triangles, ≤ 5 MB, a plain GLB (no Draco, meshopt or KTX2) for the share copy, extras.{widthM,heightM,depthM,scaleResidualM,glbVersion}. Isolation: no egress except storage. Images are decoded in the sandbox.
Credit ledger Append-only (account_id, delta, reason, ref, created_at). Reasons: PURCHASE, JOB_RESERVE, JOB_COMMIT, JOB_RELEASE, REFUND_VOIDED, GRANT. Balance = sum. Reserve on create, commit on success, release on failure (users don't pay for failed jobs). Unique (ref, reason) for idempotency.
Deletion and retention Inputs are deleted when the user keeps the result or after 30 days at most (research §1; long enough for the one free re-run), whichever comes first, and immediately on request. Outputs are deleted 30 days after success (the phone keeps its copy). DELETE /v1/account erases everything within 30 days, plus a web form. Storage region in India (latency; DPDP Act 2023 comfort). The ledger keeps only what tax law requires, pseudonymised.
Observability and cost Structured logs with no PII and no tokens. Metrics: cost per job, queue age, failure codes. Budget alerts. A server kill switch (stop accepting jobs). Per-account rate limits: 2 concurrent jobs, 10 per day. Upload caps: ≤ 200 files, ≤ 1.5 MB each, ≤ 250 MB per job.

8.2 Security and privacy for uploads (MASVS, server side)

  • Network: TLS only. Signed URLs are scoped to one object, PUT-only, with content-type and length pinned and a 15-minute TTL. Buckets have no public ACLs.
  • Play Integrity (standard request):
    • Prepared at a quiet moment, with at most 5 preparations per minute (android-performance §6.7).
    • Required on POST /scans and POST /purchases/verify, verified server-side (decodeIntegrityToken).
    • The server requires PLAY_RECOGNIZED and MEETS_DEVICE_INTEGRITY, and fails closed on those two calls because they cost money. Reads fail soft.
  • Consent:
    • Explicit per-upload consent: "Photos of this object will be uploaded to process the 3D model and deleted within 7 days". A link to the privacy policy. A tip to keep people and documents out of frame.
    • The Data safety form adds photos and videos (collected and processed) and a user ID.
  • No secrets in the APK or the public repo: the service-account key lives only in the server's secret manager. The client holds only the public API base URL.
  • Abuse: MIME sniffing and a full image decode on the server before processing; rejection of oversized or odd dimensions; rate limits; per-account and global spend caps.

8.3 Must exist before the Pro cloud feature ships (release gate)

  1. A backend with purchase verification, the ledger, RTDN, the voided-purchases job and the consume flow, tested end to end with licence testers.
  2. Auth with Google linking, plus in-app and web account deletion (Play policy).
  3. Play Integrity enforced on cost-incurring calls.
  4. Retention jobs proven: a test bucket shows inputs gone after the TTL.
  5. An updated privacy policy, the Data safety form, and a DPDP consent notice.
  6. A security review (§4.D) of the API: IDOR, signed URL scope, oversized and malicious upload, replayed integrity token and replayed purchase token, plus a dependency/CVE audit of the worker image.
  7. Cost controls: per-job cost known from 50 bench jobs, budget alerts, the kill switch, and a refund policy for failed jobs.
  8. Load test: 20 concurrent jobs and queue-age SLOs.
  9. A support path: "My scan failed / credits missing", with a ledger lookup by job id.
  10. The client behind a server-side flag, plus a staged rollout.

9. Persistence roadmap

Prerequisite (fixes R2, in P1 by Builder B): a CI job runs :data:connectedDebugAndroidTest, i.e. SpaceViewMigrationTest.

  • Option 1: a GitHub-hosted emulator through a SHA-pinned action.
  • Option 2: Gradle Managed Devices (API 34, x86_64, ATD image). :data has no ABI filter, so this works.
  • Fallback: if CI time is prohibitive, run MigrationTestHelper under Robolectric on the JVM.
  • Locally: never on the 2 GB PC.

Rules (FRIDAY §4.G):

  • v2 is frozen now that the M3 APK is on the user's phone.
  • Every shipped version gets a migration test to the latest (1→N and 2→N).
  • No destructive fallback, ever.

Scope change → one consolidated v3 for this sprint. Templates, Smart Measure and the on-device scan now all land in this sprint, so their columns are designed together and ship as one migration, MIGRATION_2_3.

  • Owner: Builder A, who adds it in the first milestone of the sprint that needs it (templates or the 3D scan).
  • Amendment window: v3 may be amended in place (documented in SpaceViewDatabase) only until any APK containing v3 is handed to the user or to testers. After that, it is v4.
Version Lands with Change Notes
v2 (shipped) M3 — Frozen
v3 This sprint (T / D / SM, whichever lands first) gallery_items: model_kind TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'PROXY_BOX' (PROXY_BOX | TEMPLATE | MESH_DEVICE | MESH_CLOUD); template_id TEXT; template_params TEXT (JSON); mesh_extent_w_m REAL, mesh_extent_h_m REAL, mesh_extent_d_m REAL, mesh_extent_w_bound95_m REAL, mesh_extent_h_bound95_m REAL, mesh_extent_d_bound95_m REAL (§7.1.5; NULL unless a mesh); mesh_info TEXT (JSON diagnostics: voxel size, depth source raw/smoothed, frames integrated, coverage %, triangles, cross-check result); capture_method TEXT (MANUAL | HANDS_FREE | AUTO_RECT | DEPTH_SCAN; NULL = legacy). measurements: capture_method TEXT. The default backfills existing rows as proxies. W/H/D stay the measured box for every kind (§7.1.5). ObjectKind.FURNITURE is an enum entry, and enumOr already tolerates unknown names. The mesh extents are typed columns, not JSON, because the UI shows them and the cross-check reads them.
v4 C (cloud) CREATE TABLE cloud_jobs (id INTEGER PK, server_job_id TEXT UNIQUE, gallery_item_id INTEGER NULL REFERENCES gallery_items(id) ON DELETE SET NULL, status TEXT NOT NULL, progress REAL, error_code TEXT, capture_dir TEXT NOT NULL, bytes_total INTEGER NOT NULL, bytes_uploaded INTEGER NOT NULL, created_at INTEGER NOT NULL, updated_at INTEGER NOT NULL) plus an index on status cloud_jobs is local state only; credits are never stored as truth on the device

Not in Room:

  • Entitlements live in their own DataStore file, excluded from backup.
  • Placement isn't persisted in M4.
  • ProCatalog is code.
  • 3D-scan drafts are files in files/shape_drafts/, swept after 24 h.

Rejected for size:

  • OpenCV (+8–15 MB per ABI [est.])
  • bundled ML Kit labelling (+5.7 MB)
  • OkHttp or Ktor for a handful of JSON calls (HttpURLConnection is enough)

Headroom to reclaim in M6: the SceneView blanket keep rules (proguard-rules.pro) and the skybox. R5 must not be undone by the skybox strip, so keep the indirect light used by Place (ADR-003).

11. ADRs

All dated 2026-11-05.

ADR-001: Placement is a separate ArFrameListener; MeasureSession is frozen in scope

  • Status: proposed.
  • Context: MeasureSession is 1000 lines (R3). The only AR host takes exactly one MeasureSession (MeasureScreen.kt:218). M4, Smart Measure and scanning all need frame access. Placement needs the gap measurement in the same world frame.
  • Options:
    • A. Extend MeasureSession. Fast, but grows a god class and risks Measure regressions.
    • B. An ArFrameListener fan-out with separate collaborators.
    • C. Separate screens and sessions. This loses the world frame and the anchors.
  • Decision: B. A listener interface and a multi-listener host. Only commitDetected(corners) (about 20 lines, SM2) may be added to MeasureSession.
  • Consequences:
    • Easier: features become isolated, JVM-testable pure cores.
    • Harder: listener order and the single primary listener for configureSession. Document both, and test the host's dispatch order.
    • Monitor: MeasureSession.kt line count in review.

ADR-002: The fit verdict combines uncertainties linearly; typed sides are exact and labelled

  • Status: proposed.
  • Context:
    • R1: the code uses RSS (FitVerdict.kt:117-126), while the M3 gate recommends linear (M3-gate.md:200).
    • Item and space errors are not known to be independent: the same phone, the same IMU scale error, and calibration shared in scope.
    • The ± model has never been validated on a device.
  • Options:
    • A. RSS. Assumes independence and is narrower, so it produces more "Fits".
    • B. Linear sum. An upper bound for any correlation ≥ −1.
    • C. RSS with an inflation factor. Arbitrary.
  • Decision: B for placement (FitPolicy.combination = LINEAR). The RSS default stays for existing callers (none in the UI today). Measured σ = bound95 / 1.96, from the rounded-up stored bound. Typed and preset sides have σ = 0 and the UI says so.
  • Consequences:
    • More "Tight" near the edge. That is the honest outcome for a "buy or not" decision.
    • Re-evaluate once the bench data shows the coverage; RSS may be justified then.
    • Boundary tests are mandatory.

ADR-003: The placed model's photo face is unlit; GLBs carry glbVersion; old models regenerate lazily

  • Status: proposed.
  • Context: R5. The material is lit PBR (GlbWriter.kt:155-161), the host has no lights (MeasureScreen.kt:228-229), and M6 plans to strip the environment. The photo already contains real lighting, so lighting it again changes its colour.
  • Options:
    • A. Add lights to the Place host only. The photo still double-lights.
    • B. Front face KHR_materials_unlit plus sides lit, plus a light and indirect light on the Place host.
    • C. Everything unlit. The box looks flat and has no depth cues.
  • Decision: B.
    • glbVersion = 2 goes in extras.
    • modelForPlacement regenerates any glbVersion < 2 model once, through the existing atomic versioned-file path.
    • M6's skybox strip must keep the Place host's indirect light.
  • Consequences:
    • The true-colour photo shows in the room, with existing data migrated without a schema change.
    • Verify on device that SceneView 4.65 and gltfio honour KHR_materials_unlit and generate mipmaps for minFilter 9987. Not checked (§14).

ADR-004: v1 Pro is verified on the client with a signature check; anything with marginal cost is verified on the server

  • Status: proposed.
  • Context:
    • There is no backend, and the repo is public (HANDOFF.md:22).
    • Pro v1 unlocks only local features: ads removal and limits.
    • Cloud credits cost real GPU money.
  • Options:
    • A. Trust the query.
    • B. A plus a local RSA signature check.
    • C. A backend now.
  • Decision: B for local entitlements. Consumables and cloud credits only with C (§8). No Play Integrity for local Pro, because without a server it is unenforceable.
  • Consequences:
    • Ships weeks earlier.
    • Accepts APK-patch piracy, which is low value.
    • Refunds take effect at the next online start.
    • When the §8 backend exists, subscriptions move to server verification as well. The BillingGateway and EntitlementRepository seams make that a data-source swap.

ADR-005: Entitlement model: a catalogue as data, a 7-day offline grace, and a lapse that never hides data

  • Status: proposed.
  • Context:
    • pro-monetization.md sets the Free/Pro split (§4.3), and it will be tuned later.
    • Users go offline.
    • Indian payments can be pending.
    • Lapsing must not punish existing data (trust, and Play's subscription policy spirit).
  • Decision:
    • ProCatalog (one Kotlin file) holds the rules. EntitlementReducer is a pure state machine (§4.4).
    • The grace period is 7 days from the last confirmation, and clock rollback never extends it.
    • The cache is excluded from backup.
    • Gates sit at entry points and at Save only, never in AR.
    • Accessibility is never paywalled.
    • When lapsed, everything already created remains usable.
  • Consequences:
    • A research decision is a one-file edit plus tests.
    • The grace period is a product knob (§13 D3).

ADR-006: Billing and Pro live in :app packages, not a new Gradle module

  • Status: proposed.
  • Context: the PC has 2 GB free, so each module costs configuration time and daemon memory. Billing types must not leak into the UI, and the logic must be testable.
  • Options:
    • A. A :billing module.
    • B. :app/billing (the Play adapter) plus :app/pro (pure domain) behind the BillingGateway interface.
  • Decision: B. A module gives no reuse benefit today, because only :app consumes entitlements. Revisit if :cloud needs entitlements; it should ask the server instead.
  • Consequences: a lint or Detekt rule (or a review check) forbids com.android.billingclient imports outside app/billing.

ADR-007: Rectangle detection is pure Kotlin in :engine/vision on a decimated luminance feed; ML Kit is used only for naming

  • Status: proposed.
  • Context:
    • The size budget rules out OpenCV.
    • ML Kit has no perspective-quad detector that is anchored in AR.
    • The existing Shapes.rectangleFromRays plus ImageProjection already turn image corners into W×H with honest ±.
    • The main-thread copy cost is R3.
  • Decision:
    • The detector runs in :engine/vision, which is deterministic and tested on synthetic and recorded images.
    • FrameImageTap (S2) copies only a decimated Y plane plus small full-resolution corner windows, into pooled buffers.
    • ML Kit labelling, unbundled, runs once per scan for naming.
  • Consequences:
    • We own a vision algorithm, so we own its tuning.
    • SM2 ships only after it beats manual on the bench (§5.3 gate).

ADR-008: One consolidated v3 for the sprint, and migration tests run in CI first

  • Status: proposed (revised for the scope change).
  • Context: R2. v2 is on a device. Templates, Smart Measure and the 3D scan now share one sprint.
  • Decision:
    • The CI emulator or managed-device migration job is a prerequisite, delivered in P1.
    • One v3 carries every column this sprint needs (§9). It may be amended only until the first APK containing it leaves the team.
    • v4 is the cloud.
    • Every shipped version gets a migration test to the latest.
  • Consequences: a single migration to test and review. CI is about 8–12 minutes longer [est.], and there is no on-device migration surprise.

ADR-009: Cloud reconstruction sits behind ReconstructionBackend (server) and CloudScanApi (client); :cloud is created only when item 5 starts

  • Status: proposed.
  • Context: the vendor and technology are still being researched (3d-capture.md). The cost is real. Play RTDN forces some Google Cloud use.
  • Decision:
    • Vendor-neutral interfaces (§7.2, §8.1).
    • Metric scale always comes from ARCore poses.
    • The output GLB contract is fixed (§8.1).
    • No client module exists until the backend exists.
  • Consequences: the vendor can be swapped, or a self-hosted pipeline used, without a client change.

ADR-010: Furniture is a parametric multi-part GLB generated in :engine

  • Status: proposed.
  • Context: shapes need to be recognisable at true size without asset downloads, and must stay within the size budget.
  • Options:
    • A. Bundled or Play Asset Delivery (PAD) sofa models, scaled. Scaling a non-uniform model distorts it, and the assets cost MB.
    • B. Parametric parts from measured parameters.
  • Decision: B.
    • Five templates.
    • Invariant: the union bounding box equals W×H×D exactly.
    • template_id is versioned by name (sofa_v1).
  • Consequences: looks "blocky but honest". Revisit PAD once a model catalogue or B2B retailer models exist (android-performance §1.5).

ADR-011: Ads are switched off at the source for Pro; AdsManager moves into AppContainer

  • Status: proposed.
  • Context: R4(a). An ads singleton outside DI starts unconditionally (MainActivity.kt:28, AdsManager.kt:383-392).
  • Decision:
    • The entitlement is injected into AdsManager.
    • Pro means no UMP and no SDK init. canShowAds includes !Pro.
    • An AdsSdk wrapper makes this testable.
    • AdsManager.get() delegates to the container, so the 10 call sites are unchanged.
  • Consequences: one place enforces "no ads for Pro". This is a HIGH-attention review item, because it touches consent.

ADR-012: Milestone order (challenges the suggested order)

  • Status: proposed, revised for the scope change. See §12.2.

ADR-013: The on-device 3D scan is a bounded dense TSDF in pure Kotlin on the CPU, and the measured box stays authoritative

  • Status: proposed.

  • Context:

    • The user moved the on-device scan into this sprint.
    • The download is 10.07 of 15 MiB.
    • There is no NDK toolchain, and the PC has only 2 GB free.
    • AR must hold 30 fps.
    • Depth-from-motion is noisy and low-resolution (the main risk, §7.1).

  • Options:

    • A. A native TSDF library (+MB, NDK).
    • B. GLES 3.1 compute in a second EGL context.
    • C. Pure-Kotlin CPU, ray-based integration in a dense volume bounded by the measured box, marching cubes, vertex clustering, and vertex colour.

  • Decision: C. Its parameters:

    • voxel = clamp(∛(V/2²¹), 1 cm, 3 cm); ≤ 12.6 MB
    • integration at 5→3→2 Hz under a governor
    • a 90 s capture limit (45–90 s target)
    • meshing with the AR session paused
    • the floor removed; the largest component kept; a user crop
    • an open, double-sided bottom
    • vertex colours, unlit

    The item's dimensions are always the measured box. The mesh extents carry their own ± and are only cross-checked. B stays as an escalation path, behind a separate decision, if the spike fails its fps criterion.

  • Consequences:

    • Zero native size. Fully JVM-testable against synthetic SDF scenes.
    • The quality ceiling is the depth input, not the algorithm.
    • "Experimental" until the device data say otherwise.
    • Monitor: fusion time per frame, thermal status and the cross-check failure rate.

ADR-014: Meshes keep the 0.5 MB-per-item budget; the GLB extras describe the GLB's own geometry

  • Status: proposed.
  • Context: the per-item budget (PLAN M3 gate) is a hard check (GalleryRepository.kt:186). Meshes are a new model kind. Placement must handle every kind.
  • Options:
    • A. A separate, larger mesh budget, for example 2 MB.
    • B. Keep 0.5 MB, with the triangle target derived from the budget.
  • Decision: B.
    • ≤ 20k triangles at about 20 B per triangle; aim for 15k.
    • No texture atlas; a 256 px WebP thumbnail.
    • extras.{widthM,heightM,depthM} always equal the GLB's own bounding box.
    • Meshes are never regenerated. If the file is unusable, placement falls back to the proxy box built from the measured dimensions.
    • KHR_mesh_quantization is the next lever, before any budget increase, and only once gltfio support is verified.
  • Consequences: one budget rule for all items, so the gallery's storage stays predictable (40 items ≈ 20 MB). A cloud mesh (§8, ≤ 8 MB) will need its own budget decision then, not now.

12. Delegation plan and milestone order

Scope change (orchestrator, 2026-11-05): this sprint now contains M4, Pro (P1/P2), Smart Measure (SM1, SM2), furniture templates (T) and the on-device 3D scan (D). SM3 (depth auto-box) and cloud stay later. The plan below rebalances the two builders, because Builder A's original queue (M4 + SM + templates + D) would be the critical path by a wide margin.

12.1 Ownership model

  • Builder A owns :ar, :data and the accuracy-critical parts of :engine, plus the AR-screen UI that drives them:

    • Engine: GlbWriter, recon, vision, FitVerdict, Plane.
    • App screens: app/place, app/scan3d, and additions to app/measure and app/scan.

    This is how M2 and M3 were split (HANDOFF.md:60,73), and it keeps each accuracy-critical vertical with one author.

  • Builder B owns the non-AR app, plus templates and naming (new for this sprint, to balance the load):

    • Pro and ads: app/pro, app/billing, app/ads, app/settings, and the paywall.
    • Templates: :engine/furniture/** (a new package; pure geometry; no overlap with A's files) and app/furniture/** (the guided flow).
    • Naming: app/scan/naming/** (ML Kit).
    • Infrastructure: AppContainer.kt, the manifest, libs.versions.toml, ci/* and the backup XMLs.
    • Later: :cloud.

  • Optional Builder C (§13 D15): :engine/recon/** only (TSDF, marching cubes, segmenter, vertex-clustering decimator, MeshExtent), worked entirely against the synthetic tests in §7.1.11.

    • It is the cheapest Gradle task (:engine:test), it touches no Android code, and it takes the largest pure-maths block off A's critical path.
    • Without C, A does it.

  • The orchestrator owns the nav/AppNavHost.kt wiring, HANDOFF.md, docs and builds (one at a time, behind the .build.lock rule).

Shared-file rules (FRIDAY §5):

File Rule
nav/AppNavHost.kt The orchestrator only. A delivers placeDestination(…) and shapeScanDestination(…). B delivers paywallDestination(…) and furnitureDestination(…), each in its own *Navigation.kt. The orchestrator adds one line per destination and replaces the onPlace Toast at AppNavHost.kt:211.
engine/GlbWriter.kt A only. A delivers the full writer API in phase 0: writeBox unlit plus glbVersion, writeParts and writeMesh, with signatures and tests. B's templates only call writeParts.
data/SpaceViewDatabase.kt, GalleryRepository.kt, GalleryItemEntity.kt, schemas A only. A lands the whole v3 (§9) in one change at the start of phase 2, including saveTemplate(TemplateDraft) for B and saveMesh(MeshDraft) for D. B never edits :data.
Strings A: strings_place.xml, strings_scan3d.xml, strings_smart.xml. B: strings_pro.xml, strings_furniture.xml, strings_naming.xml. Each under values/ and values-hi/. Nobody edits another builder's file or strings.xml. Every key has EN and HI, or lint fails.
AppContainer.kt B. A asks the orchestrator for any new member (M4 needs none; D needs none).
libs.versions.toml, ci/release-dependencies.txt, ci/allowed-permissions.txt, manifest B. Billing adds the BILLING permission; naming adds the ML Kit dependency and the DEPENDENCIES=ica meta-data. A adds no dependency this sprint.
GalleryItemScreen.kt, GalleryScreen.kt A for the Place button and the 3D badge. B for the "Add furniture" FAB and the cap counter. These are different composables in the file, so the edits are surgical and sequential: A in phase 1, B in phase 2, never at the same time.
scan/ScanReviewScreen.kt A. A inserts the naming chip that B's ObjectNamer drives (K6).
MeasureSession.kt A only. Frozen except for implements ArFrameListener (phase 0) and commitDetected (SM2).

12.2 Recommended order inside the sprint (challenges the suggested order)

The suggested order was M4 → Pro (parallel) → Smart Measure → templates → on-device scan → cloud.

Kept:

  • M4 ∥ P1 first. The files really are disjoint.
  • Cloud last, gated on Pro conversion evidence, the 3d-capture.md vendor choice and the §8.3 checklist.

Changed, with the evidence:

  1. A tiny phase 0 unblocks everyone. ArFrameListener plus ArSessionHost, and the full GlbWriter API (unlit, writeParts, writeMesh). Templates and D both produce GLBs that M4 must place (K8, K9). Defining the writer once avoids two authors editing GlbWriter.
  2. D (the on-device 3D scan) moves up, right after M4, with its device spike first.
    • It has the largest unknown in the sprint (§7.1 main risk), so it must fail fast, before the sprint commits to it.
    • It reuses M4's placement and the phase 0 seams directly.
    • The spike needs the user's depth-capable phone (§13 D17).
  3. Templates (T) run in parallel with D, on Builder B. They are deterministic, JVM-testable and isolated in new packages, and answer the core "does the sofa fit?" job (ui-ux §3.3 D).
  4. The RC and closed test start once M4, P1 and T are green. D, SM1 and SM2 ship as updates during the 14 days; the internal and closed tracks accept updates.
    • The personal Play account needs 12 testers × 14 continuous days before production (android-performance §6.7; HANDOFF.md:38). That is the longest calendar pole.
    • Billing E2E testing (P2) needs a Play-track build anyway.
  5. Smart Measure is split.
    • SM1 (hands-free plus naming) is small and low-risk.
    • SM2 (auto-rectangles) needs the bench-test data that doesn't exist yet (HANDOFF.md:68) to set its gate, so it is the sprint's last and stretch item. Pro research §1 keeps all of Smart Measure free, so it does not block Pro.
Phase Builder A Builder B (Builder C, optional) Exit gate

12.3 Contracts between the builders

# Contract Provider → consumer Shape
K1 Feature gating B → A FeatureGate.observe/check(ProFeature) (§4.3). A never imports app/billing. D checks DEVICE_SCAN before capture.
K2 No ads on AR and Place A → B Place and the shape scan use ArScreenAdGuard, and never call recordCompletedScan from Place. Saving a 3D scan does count as a completed scan (like a proxy scan), with its after-save chance used only on the next non-AR screen. B's MeasureVisitTrackerTest asserts both.
K3 Entry from Gallery A ↔ orchestrator Route place/{id: Long}; placeDestination(onBack, onOpenPaywall)
K4 Placement model A internal GalleryRepository.modelForPlacement(id): PlacementModel? (PROXY_BOX, TEMPLATE, MESH_DEVICE)
K5 Paywall entry B → A onOpenPaywall(feature: ProFeature) callback passed by nav, never a direct navigation from A's screens
K6 Naming B → A suspend fun ObjectNamer.suggest(jpeg: ByteArray): NameSuggestion?, null when the model is unavailable. A shows the chip.
K7 Cloud (later) B (:cloud) ↔ A (capture) CaptureStore.write(frame, pose, intrinsics); CloudScanRepository.enqueue(captureDir)
K8 Every GLB is placeable A (writer, :data) → A (M4) and B (T) Origin BOTTOM_CENTER (floor items, templates, meshes) or BACK_CENTER (wall items). extras.{widthM,heightM,depthM} = the GLB's own bounding box, plus glbVersion = 2 and modelKind. A mesh is never regenerated; it falls back to the proxy box (ADR-014).
K9 GLB writer API A → B GlbWriter.writeParts(parts: List<Part>, palette, origin, name): ByteArray and writeMesh(mesh: TriMesh, origin, unlit, extras): ByteArray, frozen at the end of phase 0
K10 v3 persistence A → B GalleryRepository.saveTemplate(TemplateDraft(templateId, params: Map<String, Double>, w, h, d: Dimension, label)) and saveMesh(MeshDraft(...)). Both go through the budget check and return the id.
K11 Recon API (if Builder C) C → A :engine/recon types exactly as in §7.1.4. C owns only engine/src/*/kotlin/com/spaceview/engine/recon/**.

Builder briefs must include (FRIDAY §5):

  • the docs to read (this file's section for their milestone)
  • file ownership exactly as in §12.1
  • the lock rule: mkdir .build.lock; remove it only if you created it; stale after 20 minutes
  • verification, run with the lock:
    • Builder A: :engine:test :engine:koverVerify :ar:testDebugUnitTest :data:testDebugUnitTest :app:testDebugUnitTest
    • Builder B: :app:testDebugUnitTest :app:lintDebug
    • the orchestrator does the full release build
  • the report format of FRIDAY §6.1

The build lock still allows only one Gradle run at a time, but :engine:test is the cheapest one.

If the spike's exit criteria (§7.1.10) fail, the scan stays behind a flag and the release goes ahead without it. Shipping: as "Experimental: approximate 3D shape", Free 3 saved and Pro unlimited (pro-monetization.md §1). Its dimensions always come from the measured box.

14. Not checked and sources

Not checked:

  • No builds or tests run. I ran no Gradle build and no test, as instructed. Every test count quoted comes from HANDOFF.md and M3-gate.md, not from this session.

  • Code not read in full. MeasureSession.kt lines 450–620 and 645–1000; the :app screen files beyond those named; MeasureViewModel, ScanViewModel, GalleryItemScreen, SettingsScreen and HomeScreen bodies; and engine files other than FitVerdict, GlbWriter (partial) and Shapes (signatures). R1–R5 do not depend on those, but a variant of R3 (more per-frame allocations) may exist there.

  • 3D scan specifics, unverified (no device):

    • whether raw depth is available under DepthMode.AUTOMATIC on the bench phones
    • the depth image's alignment to the CPU image and its typical resolution per phone
    • gltfio support for COLOR_0 vertex colours, doubleSided and (for a later size lever) KHR_mesh_quantization
    • the real fusion, marching-cubes and decimation timings in ART
    • thermal behaviour over 90 s of depth + AR

    The ns-per-update and timing figures in §7.1 are estimates; the spike (§7.1.10) exists to measure them.

  • 3d-capture.md was cross-checked for its on-device (§7) and cloud stack (§1–2) sections only. I did not audit its cost maths (≈ $0.45 per scan) or its licence table.

  • Device-only, unverified:

    • whether SceneView 4.65 provides a default indirect light when lights are null
    • whether gltfio honours KHR_materials_unlit and generates mipmaps for minFilter 9987
    • the actual frame time
    • the DataStore read latency at start
    • ML Kit model-download behaviour on Indian Play-services versions

  • Size figures marked [est.] are estimates. Only ML Kit's 200 KB / 5.7 MB come from Google's page. The CI bundletool gate is the authority.

  • Play policy details (Data safety rows for billing and ML Kit, account-deletion scope for anonymous accounts) need checking against the live policy at submission time.

  • pro-monetization.md landed mid-way. Its feature split, product IDs and limits are in §4.3, and its differences from this design are listed there. I did not audit its pricing or fee figures (Pro ₹129/mo, ₹799/yr, ₹149 30-day pass).

  • Lint on androidTest. I did not verify whether :data:lintDebug parses (without executing) the androidTest sources. R2 stands either way, because nothing executes the migration test.

Sources consulted this session:

  • Google Play Billing Library release notes (PBL 8.0 removals and auto-reconnection; 8.1 minSdk 23; 9.0 BILLING_UNAVAILABLE for a blocked Play Store and target 35; 9.1.0, 2026-06-18): https://developer.android.com/google/play/billing/release-notes
  • ML Kit image labelling on Android (unbundled play-services-mlkit-image-labeling ~200 KB against bundled ~5.7 MB; com.google.mlkit.vision.DEPENDENCIES = ica; 400+ labels; ModuleInstallClient): https://developers.google.com/ml-kit/vision/image-labeling/android
  • Project docs and code as cited inline (file:line).
Researchdocs/research/measurement-engine.md · updated 02 Nov 2026, 20:11

SpaceView: Measurement Engine Research

Status: research / planning only (no app code). Prepared 2026-11-02. Scope: how accurately phones can measure, how to get the most accuracy out of them, how to capture objects for the "AR Gallery", which stack to use, how many Indian phones are covered, and what the measurement engine should look like.

Bottom line. On a typical Indian mid-range ARCore phone without a depth sensor, an honest promise for lengths of 0.5–3 m is about ±2% of the length, and never better than ±1 cm (roughly ±1–2 cm at 1 m, ±3–6 cm at 3 m) under good conditions. Error gets worse with distance, featureless walls, dim light and fast motion. "±1 cm at 1 m" is a fair claim only for LiDAR iPhones/iPads (and possibly ToF Android phones), on textured surfaces, with careful technique. Do not use it in Android marketing until our own test protocol (section 6) proves it on specific devices.

For v1, use native Kotlin + ARCore SDK, rendering with SceneView/ARSceneView (Filament). Isolate the measurement core so it does not depend on the renderer. Capture objects with a "measured proxy": measured W×H×D plus a perspective-corrected photo texture, built into a textured box/plane glTF in metres. Do not use photogrammetry for v1. Later, iOS should be native Swift ARKit + RealityKit (built on GitHub Actions macOS runners), with LiDAR and Object Capture as an optional "high-fidelity" mode.

1. Realistic accuracy: ARCore vs ARKit

1.1 What the evidence actually says

The evidence is thin. No peer-reviewed study directly compares ARCore and ARKit tape-measure length error in mm over 0.5–3 m. Many figures online are vendor or marketing claims. The best numbers available:

Source Platform / sensor Finding Relevance
Luetzenburg et al., Scientific Reports 2021 (iPhone 12 Pro LiDAR) ARKit + LiDAR Small objects (>10 cm side): ±1 cm absolute accuracy. 130 m cliff: ±10 cm Best published support for "±1 cm" on LiDAR at close range
Remote Sensing Letters 2026 (iPhone 12 Pro vs 15 Pro) ARKit + LiDAR ~2 cm mean cloud-to-primitive accuracy on both models. LiDAR has not improved across generations Surface accuracy, not tape length
PMC10939328 (2024), iPhone 13 Pro distance estimation LiDAR / ARKit Mean abs. error of central-pixel distance: LiDAR 1.36 / 0.87 / 0.81 cm at 1/2/3 m. ARKit-rear 1.37 / 0.48 / 1.40 cm. Average across methods rose from 1.3 cm at 1 m to 2.4 cm at 3 m Point-distance (range) accuracy. LiDAR error does not grow over 1–3 m, but RGB/ML methods do
Benchmark of 4 proprietary VIO systems, Sensors 2022 (OptiTrack ground truth) ARKit, ARCore, T265, ZED2 ARKit most accurate and consistent, ARCore close second. Drift ≈ 0.02 m/s of motion. ARKit final drift 0.19 m in xy over a long trajectory Drift grows with path length/time, so keep measurements local and short
Feigl et al., GRAPP 2020 (large industrial hall) ARCore, ARKit, HoloLens Worst case: ~17 m drift over 120 m, scale error up to 14.4 cm per metre Shows the failure mode in large, dynamic, low-feature spaces
RealityCheck (ISM 2021) ARCore ~1.5 cm average virtual-object error vs ground truth over 22 trials Rough hint at ARCore's local consistency
ARCore depth evaluations (arXiv 2204.01693, ISPRS 2022) ARCore depth-from-motion (OnePlus 6 etc.) Per-scene depth errors of ~7–30 cm in room-scale scenes Monocular depth maps are not good enough to measure from directly. Use them only for refinement and plane fitting
Google's own Measure app ARCore Pulled from Play Store June 2021. Reviews complained of stability and inaccuracy Reputational warning: a mediocre Android measure app gets punished
RoomPlan informal (it-jim) ARKit + LiDAR 6.45 m room measured as 6.821 m (+37 cm, ~5.8%). Forum reports ~15 cm. Apple publishes only detection precision/recall (91%/90% at 3D IoU 0.3 for objects; 95% walls/windows), no cm accuracy RoomPlan is for floor plans, not certified dimensions
iOS 13 Pro scanning apps vs TLS (MDPI 2025) LiDAR apps Big differences between apps on the same device. None met survey specs. Position tracking was the main weakness App pipeline matters as much as the sensor
Reviews / blogs Apple Measure, ARCore apps Typical "±2–5 cm", rectangle auto-detect ~3% off (non-LiDAR) Unverified, but consistent with the above

1.2 Sensor-by-sensor expectations (our working estimates for 0.5–3 m)

These are engineering estimates that combine the sources above with first-principles error budgets (1.4). They are hypotheses for our test protocol to confirm or reject, not published facts.

Configuration Typical length error, good conditions Notes
ARKit + LiDAR (iPhone 12 Pro+ Pro models, iPad Pro) ~0.5–1 cm up to 2 m; ~1–2 cm at 3 m (≈0.5–1%) LiDAR range ~5 m. Works on plain walls. Weaker on glass, mirrors, black glossy surfaces (TV screens)
ARKit VIO, no LiDAR ~1–2% (1–2 cm at 1 m) Scale from IMU+camera. Plane hit-tests are good on textured surfaces
ARCore + hardware ToF (few, older Samsung S10 5G / S20+ / Note10+, LG V60; almost none current in India) ~1–1.5% Depth API fuses ToF. Also works on low-texture surfaces
ARCore + Depth API (depth-from-motion), no ToF ~1.5–2.5% Depth helps where there is no plane, but raw depth error at room scale is cm-to-dm. Use it only for robust plane fitting near the target
ARCore VIO + plane hit-tests only ~2–3%; worse on white walls Vertical planes on plain painted walls (common in Indian homes and cafés) often fail to detect at all
ARCore Instant Placement Do not use for measurement Scale-less until it switches to full tracking. Distance is an assumed value (sample uses 2.0 m)
RoomPlan (LiDAR) whole-room ~2–6% of wall length (anecdotal) Good for layout. Use direct point-to-point for exact numbers

1.3 How error grows with distance

There are three separate effects:

  1. Pixel / endpoint localisation, which grows linearly with distance Z. One image pixel covers about Z / f_px. With ARCore's default 640×480 CPU image and ~65° horizontal FOV, f ≈ 500 px, so 1 px ≈ 2 mm at 1 m, 4 mm at 2 m, 6 mm at 3 m. A finger tap on a phone screen is accurate to several camera pixels at best. This is why we need a magnifier loupe, edge snapping, and a high-resolution CPU image stream (CameraConfig) where the device supports one.
  2. Depth / plane-distance bias, which acts as a proportional error. If the surface is estimated δZ too far away, every length on it is scaled by (1 + δZ/Z). A 1 cm plane bias at 1 m means 1% length error. Monocular depth bias grows roughly with Z², so it gets worse fast beyond 2 m. LiDAR is roughly constant within 0.3–3 m (see the PMC table).
  3. VIO scale error and drift, which grow with path length and time, not with Z directly. A short, still measurement taken from one viewpoint has little drift. Walking 4 m along a wall to measure it builds up drift (Sensors 2022: ~2 cm/s of movement in hard cases).

Rule of thumb: relative error is roughly constant (≈1–3%) when the whole object is in view from one spot. It rises when (a) you are > 2.5–3 m away (pixel and depth limits), (b) you must walk to see both endpoints (drift), or (c) the surface is low-texture.

1.4 Worked error budget: 60 × 40 cm photo frame on a café wall, 1.5 m, mid-range ARCore phone, no ToF

Term Estimate (1σ) Effect on 60 cm width
Endpoint localisation, 2 ends, edge-snapped to ~1 px (VGA, f≈500) 3 mm/endpoint √2 × 3 ≈ 4.2 mm
Wall-plane distance bias (fitted plane, textured wall) ~1–1.5 cm at 1.5 m (0.7–1%) 4–6 mm
VIO metric scale error ~1% (estimate) 6 mm
RSS total ≈ 9–10 mm (≈1.6%). 95% band ≈ ±2 cm

With a high-resolution CPU stream (e.g. 1920×1080, f ≈ 1500 px) the localisation term drops to ~1.5 mm. With reference-object scale calibration (2.6) the scale term can fall to ~0.3–0.5%. That brings the total to ~0.8–1%, which is the realistic ceiling for non-LiDAR Android.

1.5 Honest claims we can make

  • Android (non-ToF): "Typically within ±2% (about ±1–2 cm at arm's length) in good light on textured surfaces. Each measurement shows its own confidence range." Promise nothing better until our protocol shows it per device tier.
  • LiDAR iPhone: "Typically within ±1 cm up to ~2 m" is supported by Luetzenburg 2021 and PMC10939328, but still must be verified for our own pipeline.
  • Never claim "laser-accurate". Laser distance meters are ±1.5–2 mm. Apple itself calls Measure "approximate", and Google withdrew its own Measure app.
  • Always display value ± uncertainty plus a quality badge, and refuse to save "precise" records when the uncertainty is above a threshold.

2. Techniques to maximise accuracy

Listed in rough order of payoff:

  1. Tracking-quality gating (mandatory). Accept samples only when Camera.trackingState == TRACKING and trackingFailureReason == NONE. Also gate on gyro angular velocity (reject motion blur, e.g. > ~0.3 rad/s), on scene brightness (ARCore light estimate / mean pixel intensity), and on feature count in the ROI. Make an initialisation phase mandatory: a "move the phone side to side" prompt until tracking is stable and a plane is found, because parallax gives VIO its scale.
  2. Hit-test priority and filtering. Prefer, in order: (a) Plane hits with isPoseInPolygon on a TRACKING plane that is not subsumed; (b) DepthPoint hits (Depth API enabled, raw-depth confidence above threshold); (c) feature Point hits with a surface normal. Never accept InstantPlacementPoint for measurements. Hit results come back sorted by distance; take the first valid one of the preferred type.
  3. Own plane fitting near the target. Don't trust ARCore's plane polygon alone. Collect raw-depth points (acquireRawDepthImage16Bits / acquireRawDepthImageMeters from ARCore 1.56, with confidence ≥ ~200/255) plus ARCore feature points inside the region of interest. Fit a plane with RANSAC + least squares and project tapped or snapped 2D points onto it by ray–plane intersection. This is the key technique for wall-hung frames and TVs. It also gives a plane-fit residual that feeds the uncertainty estimate.
  4. Multi-sample averaging per endpoint. While the reticle is held on a point, collect N ≈ 15–30 samples over ~0.5–1 s, ideally with a small parallax sweep. Use a robust estimator (median or trimmed mean). The sample spread gives a per-endpoint covariance. Report σ and reject if it is above a threshold.
  5. Anchor endpoints and recompute at save time. Create an Anchor per endpoint. ARCore refines anchor poses as its map improves, so compute final distances from current anchor poses, and recompute when the user taps "save". Show the value converging ("stabilising…").
  6. Edge and corner snapping (computer vision). Detect line segments and quadrilaterals in the camera image around the reticle: OpenCV LSD/Canny + Hough, or a contour-quad detector for frames and TVs. Snap to sub-pixel corners (cornerSubPix). Ask for the high-resolution CPU stream via Session.getSupportedCameraConfigs() / setCameraConfig() (default is VGA 640×480). Rectangle mode: detect the 4 corners of a frame or TV, then intersect each corner ray with the fitted wall plane to get W and H in one shot. Enforce right angles and parallel sides as a consistency check (deviation feeds uncertainty).
  7. Distance coaching. Show live guidance for the target's best distance: "move closer" when > 2 m for objects under 1 m; "step back" when the object doesn't fit in the frame. Get the whole object into one view so there is no walking drift. Recommend a near-perpendicular view to the surface (< 30° obliquity), because oblique views increase depth bias.
  8. Scale-drift handling. For spans that need walking (room dimensions, long walls), use an anchor chain: drop intermediate anchors, keep the path short, and encourage a loop back to the start to trigger loop closure. Show a larger uncertainty. Above ~5 m, recommend a laser meter or "measure in segments".
  9. Reference-object calibration (optional "precision mode"). The user puts a known object on the same plane as the target and measures it: A4 sheet 297 × 210 mm, or ISO/IEC 7810 ID-1 card 85.60 × 53.98 mm. The scale factor s = true/measured corrects VIO scale for that plane and session. Apply it only if 0.9 < s < 1.1, otherwise flag a tracking problem. Caveats: a card is too small at distance (at 1.5 m it spans ~30 VGA px, so a 1 px error ≈ 3%). Use A4 for anything beyond ~50 cm, and use the high-res stream. The same reference supports a single-photo homography mode (no AR needed): with a full-res still (f ≈ 3000 px), the A4's 4 corners define the plane's metric homography, and the target's corners on that plane are measured directly. Error grows when extrapolating to objects much larger than the reference, so give it an uncertainty from corner-localisation error propagation. This mode is also the fallback for non-ARCore phones (section 5).
  10. Uncertainty estimation shown to the user. Propagate per-endpoint covariance, plane-fit residual, a distance-dependent pixel term, a device-tier scale prior (from our test data), and a tracking-quality penalty. Show "60.4 cm ± 0.9 cm". Colour-code: green ≤1%, amber ≤3%, red >3%. Calibrate the displayed ± in the test protocol so that ~90% of true values really fall inside the band. A ± that is honest is worth more than one that is small.
  11. Repeat-and-agree. For "save to Gallery", require 2 independent measurements (e.g. re-aim) that agree within the shown uncertainty. Save the mean.
  12. Environment hints. Warn on low light, plain white walls (low feature count), glass, mirrors and glossy black TV screens. For TVs, snap to the bezel or outer frame, not the screen reflection.

3. Object capture: photogrammetry vs "measured proxy"

3.1 Options

Option Platform Requirements / status Dimensional fidelity
Apple Object Capture (ObjectCaptureSession + PhotogrammetrySession, RealityKit) iOS 17+ on device, macOS On iOS: LiDAR devices with a strong GPU (A14+ per WWDC23 sample; Apple DTS says LiDAR is effectively required). macOS: GPU ≥4 GB + ray tracing. Output USDZ Metric scale from LiDAR/ARKit. Good for 3D objects with texture; ~1–2 cm class. Weak on glossy, transparent, thin, featureless objects. iOS 26 / iPhone 17 Pro failures reported by developers (unverified)
Android native equivalent none No Google equivalent of Object Capture n/a
Third-party cloud photogrammetry (KIRI Engine API, Polycam API; RealityScan app has no public API) Upload photos, then server KIRI: up to 200 photos/call, ~3–5 min processing. Cost per scan, needs network, privacy considerations Photo-only reconstruction is scale-ambiguous. It needs a scale bar or ARCore camera poses to be metric. Vendors' "sub-cm" claims are for LiDAR modes
Self-hosted server (COLMAP / OpenMVS / Meshroom, or Gaussian splatting) with ARCore poses as priors Android, then our server Server infra, GPU cost, long processing Can be metric if ARCore poses/anchors are used. Fiddly. Same reflective-surface weaknesses
Measured proxy (our recommendation) Any ARCore phone; also the non-AR fallback On device, instant, offline Exactly as good as the measurement (±1–2%). The texture is a perspective-rectified photo

3.2 Why the proxy wins for frames, TVs and appliances

  • These objects are planar or box-shaped. A box with exact W×H×D and good front-face texture is visually convincing when placed against a wall, and it is dimensionally exact by construction.
  • Photogrammetry is weakest on exactly these objects: glass in frames, glossy black TV panels, uniform white appliance panels. These give holes and wobbly edges, so the mesh's bounding box differs from the true size by more than the measurement error. Cropping a mesh and estimating a bounding box adds more error.
  • What the user cares about is "does it fit my wall / cabinet". That is the bounding box. A proxy gives the bounding box directly; photogrammetry gives it only indirectly.
  • It needs no server, no per-scan cost, and works offline (good for Indian connectivity), with an instant result.

3.3 Recommended capture pipeline (v1 Android)

  1. Detect the rectangle (CV quad + AR plane) and measure W×H on the fitted plane, as in section 2.
  2. Measure depth D:
    • Wall-hung (frame, TV): offer presets (thin frame 2 cm, canvas 4 cm, TV ~ model lookup) or a side-view 2-point measurement. Mark D as "low confidence", because a 2–5 cm protrusion is comparable to the error on non-depth phones.
    • Free-standing (appliance): 3-point box. Two base points on the floor plane give W and D, and a vertical height point gives H.
  3. Grab a high-res still (the ARCore CPU image at max config, or a CameraX still if switching is acceptable). Unwarp the front face with the homography from the 4 detected corners into a straight-on texture (e.g. 1024 px on the long side). Optionally capture side and top textures, or use solid colour sampled from the edges.
  4. Build the model. Front face = texture, sides = edge colour, optional bevel. Real-world units 1 unit = 1 m. Save as glTF 2.0 / GLB (Scene Viewer / Filament native). Keep it under 10 MB and under 50k triangles.
  5. Save the canonical record, not just the mesh: {dims_m, uncertainty_m, per-dim confidence, device model, ARCore version, tracking stats, calibration used, textures, capture photos, timestamps}. Meshes are generated from this record per platform (GLB on Android, USDZ on iOS), so later re-export or upgrade is possible.

3.4 Later

  • iOS LiDAR "High-fidelity scan" mode with Object Capture for real 3D objects (vases, chairs). Snap its mesh's bounding box to the measured proxy dims, or show both.
  • Android: optional cloud photogrammetry for 3D objects, using ARCore poses to fix scale. Only after the proxy flow is proven.

4. Stack recommendation

4.1 Android (v1)

Recommended: native Kotlin + ARCore SDK (com.google.ar:core, current 1.57.0, Oct 2026) + SceneView arsceneview (current ~4.65.0; Jetpack Compose + Filament; Apache-2.0; Android "Stable").

  • Sceneform is deprecated (Google archived it in 2020). SceneView is its community successor and the de facto current choice. It needs minSdk 24, compileSdk 37, AGP 9.4+, Kotlin 2.4, JVM target 21. This matches the dev's JDK 21 CLI + Gradle + GitHub Actions setup with no Android Studio.
    • Risk: SceneView is largely one maintainer (Thomas Gorisse) and has fast-moving versions. Mitigation: keep the measurement engine renderer-agnostic. It talks to the ARCore Session/Frame directly, and SceneView is only the view layer. If SceneView stalls, swap to raw Filament or OpenGL ES.
  • Raw ARCore + OpenGL ES (Google's hello_ar_kotlin sample pattern) gives full control and has no third-party dependency, but means writing your own rendering, glTF loading and occlusion. It is a viable fallback, but slower to build for the Gallery/placement features.
  • ARCore for Jetpack XR (androidx.xr.arcore, still alpha) runs on phones through Play Services for AR, but lacks some features and is aimed at Android XR headsets. Not for v1.
  • Unity AR Foundation is ruled out (no Unity, heavy licence and tooling), and has no accuracy advantage: it wraps the same ARCore/ARKit.
  • Scene Viewer (Google's intent-based AR viewer, glTF/GLB, resizable=false locks true scale, mode=ar_only) is a zero-code way to "place in room at true scale" for prototyping or as a fallback viewer. However, it needs ARCore and the Google app, and only offers basic UX. Use it for the early demo, then build in-app placement with SceneView.
  • Computer vision: OpenCV Android SDK (LSD, contours, cornerSubPix, homography) inside a pure-Kotlin/JNI module.

4.2 iOS (later)

Native Swift + ARKit + RealityKit, built and signed on GitHub Actions macOS runners (no Mac locally). Use:

  • ARWorldTrackingConfiguration with .sceneDepth / .smoothedSceneDepth on LiDAR devices, and raycasts for non-LiDAR devices.
  • Vision VNDetectRectanglesRequest for frame and TV quads.
  • RoomPlan for room outline (with the caveat about its accuracy).
  • ObjectCaptureSession for the LiDAR high-fidelity mode.
  • Quick Look / RealityKit to place USDZ.

The math core (filters, plane fit, uncertainty, record schema) could be shared through Kotlin Multiplatform (KMP builds an XCFramework on a macOS runner). Alternatively, port it as a small, well-tested Swift module. Either way, the test vectors (recorded inputs and expected outputs) are what must be shared.

  • SceneView's iOS port is still Alpha, so don't rely on it for iOS.

4.3 What "REA" probably means

There is no mainstream AR framework called "REA". Plausible readings, most likely first:

  1. Apple's "Reality" stack: RealityKit / Reality Composer (Pro) / .reality files. This is the most natural reading of "use REA if needed" in an AR app that will later go to iOS. Best guess. It maps to "use RealityKit on iOS".
  2. React Native AR, i.e. ViroReact (ReactVision, maintained in 2025, New-Architecture support, ARCore + ARKit), or "REA" as the class prefix of React Native Reanimated. That would imply a cross-platform JS UI. For a precision measurement engine this adds a bridge layer and hides ARCore/ARKit details we need (raw depth, camera configs, anchors). It is acceptable only for non-AR UI screens.
  3. Google's Scene Viewer / <model-viewer> (web AR viewer), if "REA" was a typo for a viewer component.
  4. University of Ulm's "AREA" research framework (location-based AR). Unlikely.

Action: confirm with the user. Whatever it means, the measurement engine should stay native.

5. ARCore device coverage in India and fallback

  • Market: Android was 89.6% of Indian smartphone shipments in Q3 2025 (IDC). The market was ~152 M units in 2025 (flat). ~1 in 5 phones shipped was "premium" (Counterpoint). iPhone share is rising, but LiDAR is only on iPhone Pro / iPad Pro, so LiDAR-class accuracy reaches a small single-digit % of the market.
  • ARCore certified list (Google Play): Google certifies each model for motion tracking (camera + IMU quality, calibration, CPU). The live list has hundreds of models from the brands that dominate India: Xiaomi/Redmi/POCO (100+), Vivo/iQOO (150+), Oppo/OnePlus (150+), Samsung incl. Galaxy A/M/F (100+), Motorola (~110), Realme (~60), Tecno/Infinix/itel (~50 each), Nothing (~10). Lava is absent. Many 2024–2025 mid-rangers are marked "Supports Depth API", e.g. Redmi Note 14, POCO X7 Pro, realme 15 Pro, iQOO Z10, vivo T4 Pro, Oppo F29, Galaxy A56/M36, moto g84, Nord 5, Nothing Phone (3a). Older and budget models (e.g. Realme 5/6/7 Pro) often lack Depth. Hardware ToF entries are old Samsung/LG flagships and practically irrelevant in India now.
  • No public figure for "% of Indian active devices that are ARCore-certified." Estimate: most ₹12k+ phones from 2022 onward are covered, and sub-₹10k models and some regional brands are spotty. Measure it ourselves: Play Console device-catalogue reach for the AR-required feature, plus in-app telemetry of ArCoreApk.checkAvailability() results.
  • Requirements: Android 7.0+ (API 24), Google Play Services for AR (auto-installed / updated via Play).

Fallback design (declare ARCore as "AR Optional" in the manifest so the app installs on every phone):

  1. Reference-photo measure (no AR): the user places an A4 sheet or card on the same plane, takes a photo, and the app computes a homography to measure flat objects. Accuracy can be ~1% for objects up to ~2× the reference size. Show a larger uncertainty beyond that.
  2. Manual entry: type dimensions from a tape or the product spec (e.g. a TV model lookup), plus a photo, to create a Gallery proxy.
  3. Gallery 3D viewer (Filament / SceneView 3D mode, no ARCore) and "place on a photo of your room" 2D compositing, using a reference object in the room photo for scale.
  4. Uncertified phones that still run ARCore via sideloaded services: don't support them. Accuracy is unknown, and Google notes uncertified devices may give a "far from perfect" experience.

6. Proposed measurement-engine architecture

6.1 Modules

:measure-core        (pure Kotlin/JVM, no Android deps; unit-testable on CI; KMP-ready)
  geometry/          ray-plane intersection, RANSAC plane fit, homography, quad fitting
  estimation/        robust averaging, covariance, uncertainty propagation, scale calibration
  model/             MeasuredObject record schema (JSON), units, versioning
  quality/           gating rules, quality score, thresholds per device tier

:measure-arcore      (Android; adapts ARCore Session/Frame to core types)
  FrameSampler       pulls pose, intrinsics, tracking state, light estimate, depth+confidence, CPU image
  HitResolver        hit-test priority (Plane > DepthPoint > Point; never InstantPlacement)
  DepthPlaneFitter   raw depth (meters API, conf>=thr) + feature points -> local plane
  AnchorStore        endpoint anchors; recompute on map refinement
  CameraConfigPicker highest CPU-image resolution that keeps 30 fps

:measure-vision      (OpenCV via JNI or Java bindings)
  EdgeSnapper        LSD / Canny near reticle, sub-pixel corner refine
  QuadDetector       frame/TV rectangle detection + tracking across frames
  Rectifier          homography unwarp -> front-face texture

:measure-modes       (state machines, UI-agnostic)
  PointToPoint, Rectangle (frame/TV), Box (appliance), Height (floor->point),
  RoomSpan (anchor chain), ReferenceCalibration, PhotoReference (non-AR)

:capture-proxy       builds textured box/plane GLB from MeasuredObject (1 unit = 1 m)
:gallery-store       Room DB + files: records, textures, GLB; export/import
:app-ui              Compose + SceneView ARSceneView (reticle, loupe, live value ± unc, badges)
:testkit             recorded-session replay harness, ground-truth catalogue, metrics reports

6.2 Data flow (one measurement)

ARCore Frame ──► FrameSampler ──► QualityGate ──(reject: coach user)──┐
                      │                 │ pass                        │
                      ▼                 ▼                             │
               CPU image (hi-res)   HitResolver ◄── reticle / snapped px ◄── EdgeSnapper/QuadDetector
                      │                 │
                      │                 ▼
                      │          DepthPlaneFitter (local plane, residual)
                      │                 │
                      ▼                 ▼
                 Rectifier       Ray-plane intersection ─► sample buffer (N≈15–30)
                      │                 │
                      │                 ▼
                      │          RobustEstimator ─► endpoint + covariance ─► Anchor
                      │                 │
                      │                 ▼
                      │          Measurement (value, σ, quality) ◄── ScaleCalibration (optional)
                      │                 │
                      ▼                 ▼
               texture.png      MeasuredObject record ─► ProxyBuilder ─► model.glb ─► Gallery

Placement later: load GLB at scale 1.0. Scaling gestures are locked by default (true scale is the product promise). Anchor it on a detected plane, and show the dims ± uncertainty as a label.

6.3 Accuracy test protocol

Ground truth

  • Reference instrument: laser distance meter (±1.5 mm class) plus a steel tape / 1 m steel rule. Measure each GT object 3×, use the mean, and record temperature-independent values.
  • Object set (keep it physically in a "test kit" box):
    • A4 sheet 297×210 mm; ID-1 card 85.60×53.98 mm
    • 2 photo frames (e.g. 30×40 cm and 50×70 cm), one glossy glass, one matte
    • A TV (e.g. 43", ~96×56 cm outer) switched off (glossy black)
    • A box appliance (microwave ~45×26×35 cm) and a cardboard box with known dims
    • A door (~0.9×2.1 m) and 2 wall lengths (~3 m, ~5 m) for room-span mode
    • Height: a person/pole of known height (~1.7 m)

Conditions matrix (subset per release, full matrix per milestone)

  • Distance: 0.5, 1, 2, 3 m. View angle: 0°, 30°, 45°.
  • Light: bright (>500 lux), home evening (~100–150 lux), mixed or backlit (window).
  • Surface: textured wall, plain white/painted wall, glossy surface.
  • Devices (tiers): T0 reference Pixel; T1 Depth-API mid-rangers (Redmi Note, Galaxy A5x, realme, vivo/iQOO, moto g); T2 non-Depth budget ARCore phone; T3 LiDAR iPhone Pro (iOS phase); T4 non-LiDAR iPhone (iOS phase).
  • Repetitions: 10 per condition × 3 operators (including one first-time user, to capture UX-driven error).

Metrics

  • Signed error (bias), absolute error (mm), relative error (%), median, P95, repeatability (SD of repeats), between-operator reproducibility.
  • Uncertainty calibration: fraction of trials where GT lies inside the displayed ± band (target 85–95%; too high means the band is too wide to be useful).
  • Reject/abstain rate: the share of attempts where the engine refused or warned. Low error achieved by refusing everything is a fail.
  • Time-to-measure.

Pass/fail targets for v1 (Android), conditions: 0.5–2 m, good light, textured surface

Tier Median abs. error P95 abs. error Uncertainty coverage Abstain rate
T0 / T1 (Depth API) ≤ 1.0% or ≤ 6 mm (whichever larger) ≤ 2.5% 85–95% ≤ 15%
T2 (no Depth) ≤ 1.5% or ≤ 8 mm ≤ 3.5% 85–95% ≤ 25%
Any tier, 3 m or plain wall or dim report only; must warn and widen ± — ≥ 80% —
T3 LiDAR (iOS phase) ≤ 0.6% or ≤ 4 mm ≤ 1.5% 85–95% ≤ 10%
Rectangle mode, frames/TVs (all tiers) W and H each meet the tier targets above, and the corner right-angle deviation is ≤ 2°
Depth D of wall-hung objects not gated in v1 (labelled "estimate")

Release gate: no regression of more than 10% in median or P95 against the previous release on the replay set.

Regression automation

  • Use the ARCore Recording & Playback API to record sessions (MP4 with camera, IMU and depth tracks) for every GT trial. Replay them through the engine on a test device in CI-like runs and compare against stored GT. ARCore playback needs a physical device, so run on a dev phone or a device-farm phone. Pure :measure-core logic gets JVM unit tests with synthetic data in GitHub Actions.
  • Keep a versioned GT catalogue (JSON) and generate an HTML/CSV report per build.

6.4 Open risks

  1. Plain painted walls (very common): vertical planes may not be detected, and depth-from-motion is weak there. This hits the core "frame on café wall" use case. Mitigations: rely on the frame's own texture and edges for depth points, 3-tap plane, reference-sheet mode.
  2. Scale error on budget phones with poor IMU calibration. Is the certified list's quality enough for 1.5%? Unknown until tested. Calibration mode helps.
  3. Depth (D) of flat objects cannot be measured reliably without LiDAR/ToF.
  4. SceneView bus factor (single maintainer, very frequent breaking upgrades of AGP/Kotlin/compileSdk). Isolate it and pin versions.
  5. Unknown ARCore coverage share in India. Instrument from day 1, and keep the non-AR fallback first-class.
  6. Glossy and transparent surfaces (glass frames, TV screens) cause bad hits. Snap to bezels and frames, and warn.
  7. iOS without a Mac: signing, provisioning and on-device testing via GitHub Actions + TestFlight only; slow iteration. LiDAR test devices are needed for T3 validation.
  8. Expectation management: competitor apps claim "1 cm accuracy". Our honest ± display may look worse in screenshots, but it is the defensible position. Google withdrew its own Measure app over accuracy complaints.
