Expectations [est.]:

  • Free-to-paid conversion for Indian utility apps is typically 0.5–2%.
  • At 10k MAU, that is roughly 50–200 Pro users, or ₹4k–20k MRR. Still small, so B2B remains primary.
  • Use Google Play's India regional pricing and UPI Autopay-friendly subscriptions.
  • Offer a 7-day trial on annual plans.

7. Key risks and a 12-month go-to-market

7.1 Risks and mitigations

Risk Detail Mitigation
Accuracy liability Peer-reviewed tests show iPhone LiDAR reaching ±1 cm on small objects but ~10 cm errors on room- or building-scale scans (iPhone 12 Pro evaluation in Scientific Reports, as summarised in search results; ISPRS 2024; SAE 2021). Non-depth ARCore is worse and varies by device. "Exact dimensions" marketing invites disputes when a sofa or TV doesn't fit Never claim "exact". Show ± tolerance on every measurement. Build in a fit margin: fit-check flags "tight" within 5 cm. Run a calibration check with an A4 sheet or credit card. Put "indicative, verify before purchase / construction" in the ToS. B2B contracts cap liability. Publish an in-house accuracy benchmark per device class
Device fragmentation Only ARCore-certified devices work (Google device list). Google says >88% of active ARCore devices support the Depth API (May 2026), but low-end Indian phones and non-certified models are excluded. LiDAR is iPhone Pro / iPad Pro only, a small slice of Apple's 9–10% India share Web embed works without an install, and tape-entry fit check works on any phone. Graceful fallback from depth to plane-only to manual entry. Test on the top 20 Indian models (Redmi, Samsung A/M, Vivo, Realme, Oppo). Android-first
Retailer sales cycles 6–12+ months at national chains; InfoSec, DPDP, procurement; pilots stalling without an internal champion Lead with mid-market and D2C (1–3 months). Use paid pilots with pre-agreed success criteria. Self-serve Shopify tier for cash flow. Pick a category-head champion, not IT
Incumbents and in-house teams Reliance (Fynd/GlamAR, Urban Ladder), Flipkart (Scapic), Pepperfry (Avataar), Amazon global Avoid head-on. Compete on parametric zero-cost SKUs, fit-check UX, price and speed. Treat Avataar's pivot to video as an opening
Low AR usage Baymard: 6% proactive use Make the fit verdict the hero, not AR. Use saved room profiles. Put a "fits ✓" badge on PDPs. Make AR optional
Ad policy / account risk AdMob policy strikes can suspend revenue Conservative caps (section 2.3), no ads on the camera screen, policy review before each release
Privacy (DPDP) Room imagery is personal data. Rules phase in through about May 2027 On-device processing; store geometry, not photos, by default; explicit consent; DPA with retailers; no ad SDK in B2B builds
Solo-founder bandwidth AR, cross-platform, B2B sales and 3D production in parallel Android plus web only for 6 months. Outsource 3D. Make one channel (electronics) the focus. Find a co-founder or sales partner by month 6

7.2 Recommended 12-month phased GTM

Phase 1, months 0–3: build the wedge and get proof of accuracy

  • Android consumer MVP (ARCore): measure (with ± tolerance), save object to the AR Gallery, place at true scale, plus saved room/wall profiles.
  • Parametric library: generic TVs 32"–98" (with stand or wall-mount), fridges, washing machines, ACs, standard bed and mattress sizes.
  • SEO and content: "Will a 65-inch TV fit my wall?" / "TV size for viewing distance" calculator pages, a web version using <model-viewer>, and YouTube Shorts / Instagram Reels demos.
  • Ads: capped plan from section 2.3 plus rewarded ads. No Pro tier yet.
  • Publish an accuracy benchmark across 10 popular devices.
  • Targets [est.]: 10–25k installs, D7 retention ≥15%, a working web fit-check demo.

Phase 2, months 3–6: monetize consumers and recruit design partners

  • Launch Pro (₹99/mo, ₹599/yr): ad-free, unlimited gallery, PDF floor plans, GLB/USDZ export.
  • Build the B2B web embed v1: spec-feed CSV → parametric GLB → <model-viewer> + fit-check, plus an analytics dashboard.
  • Recruit 3–5 design partners (free or low-cost): 1–2 regional electronics chains, 1 TV/appliance brand, 1–2 Shopify D2C furniture or mattress brands.
  • List a Shopify app (Starter tier).
  • Apply to Flipkart Leap Ahead or similar accelerators.

Phase 3, months 6–9: paid pilots and case studies

  • Convert design partners into paid pilots (₹1–3 lakh) with A/B-tested KPIs (section 4.3).
  • Add a furniture 3D modelling service through an outsourced studio partner.
  • Launch an iOS app with a LiDAR high-accuracy mode.
  • Launch the Pro for Business tier for interior designers and sellers.
  • Publish 1–2 co-branded case studies.

Phase 4, months 9–12: scale B2B

  • Launch the Starter, Growth and Enterprise SaaS tiers. Pitch national players (Croma, Vijay Sales, Godrej, Wakefit) using the case studies.
  • Release Android SDK v1 for retailer apps; build in-store mode for one chain.
  • Run an exploratory pilot with one movers platform (volume estimation) if a partner appears.
  • In the consumer app, replace generic ads with partner-sponsored placements where possible.
  • Year-1 targets [est.]:
    • 50–100k installs and 5–10k DAU (ads ≈ ₹25–50k/mo).
    • 100–300 Pro subscribers.
    • 3–6 paying B2B customers, ₹1–3 lakh MRR.
    • At least one national-chain or brand conversation in procurement.

7.3 Year-1 revenue mix (base case) [est.]

Stream Month-12 run-rate
Consumer ads (≈7k DAU) ₹30–40k/mo
Pro + Pro for Business ₹20–60k/mo
B2B SaaS (Starter + Growth) ₹1–2.5 lakh/mo
Pilots + 3D modelling (one-off) ₹3–8 lakh cumulative

Bottom line:

  • The consumer app is the demo, data and distribution engine. Ads only cover server costs.
  • The business is B2B fit-check / AR for size-sensitive retail, won on zero-cost parametric SKUs for electronics and appliances, with furniture modelling and designer subscriptions as the second leg.
Researchdocs/research/android-performance.md · updated 02 Nov 2026, 20:17

SpaceView: Android performance, size and Play Store readiness research

Status: research for the planning phase. No app code yet. Date: 2 November 2026 Scope: native Kotlin + Jetpack Compose + ARCore + a Filament-based renderer (probably SceneView; another agent is choosing it). AdMob for revenue. India first, so many 3–4 GB RAM phones, slow eMMC storage and costly mobile data. Android only, on Google Play. Built with a CLI toolchain and GitHub Actions; Android Studio is not used.

Each section ends with a short "Do" list. Section 0 collects all targets and budgets in one place, and Section 10 lists the sources. Any claim not confirmed by an official source is labelled (unverified).

0. Summary: targets and budgets

Metric SpaceView target ("top-notch") Hard limit / Play threshold
Play download size, arm64 phone (bundletool get-size total) ≤ 15 MB at launch, stretch goal ≤ 12 MB; CI fails above 18 MB Play warns mobile-data users above 200 MB. Base module max 500 MB
Installed size, before user data ≤ 45 MB —
Extra one-off download of Google Play Services for AR (on phones that lack it) ~36–38 MB, not counted in our size but users see it —
Cold start TTID, mid-range phone (P50) ≤ 800 ms; ≤ 1.5 s on a 3 GB low-end phone Vitals counts cold ≥ 5 s, warm ≥ 2 s, hot ≥ 1.5 s as excessive
Time from tapping "Measure" to first tracked AR frame ≤ 1.5 s to camera preview, plus tracking time (depends on the scene) —
AR frame rate Steady 30 fps (camera locked to 30) with no dropped frames; UI thread frames < 16 ms —
User-perceived crash rate < 0.3 % overall 1.09 % overall, 8 % per phone model
User-perceived ANR rate < 0.15 % overall 0.47 % overall, 8 % per phone model
Foreground memory (Anon RSS + swap) during AR, P99 ≤ 700 MB on 4 GB phones; ≤ 500 MB on 3 GB phones 2.0 GB P99 for the 4 GB tier. No threshold for phones under 4 GB, but low-memory kills hit there first
Bitmap memory ≤ 100 MB 200 MB (perceptible/background)
Excessive partial wake locks 0 (use no wake locks; use FLAG_KEEP_SCREEN_ON) 5 %
Saved gallery item (GLB + thumbnail) ≤ 500 KB typical, ≤ 1.5 MB max —
GPU texture memory per placed object ≤ 6 MB (1024² RGBA8 with mips ≈ 5.3 MB) —
Continuous AR session Device stays below THERMAL_STATUS_MODERATE for at least 10 min on a mid-range phone; automatic quality step-down beyond that —

Must-do items (ranked)

  1. target SDK 36 now. It has been required for new apps since 31 Aug 2026. Plan target 37 before Aug 2027.
  2. 16 KB page size: every .so (ARCore, Filament/gltfio, anything else) must have 16 KB-aligned ELF segments. Play blocks updates that fail this from 1 Feb 2027. Add a CI check from the first commit.
  3. R8 full mode plus resource shrinking on release builds, with narrow keep rules and no blanket -keep class com.google.android.filament.** { *; }.
  4. Baseline and Startup Profiles, generated on CI with a Gradle Managed Device (AOSP image, KVM on ubuntu runners).
  5. Defer heavy initialisation: no ARCore, Filament or AdMob work in Application.onCreate. Initialise AdMob on a background thread after UMP consent.
  6. AR session hygiene: 30 fps camera config, depth and HDR lighting off by default, plane finding off after placement, session.pause() in onPause and close() in onDestroy, every CPU Image closed inside use {}.
  7. Thermal step-down with PowerManager thermal status and headroom.
  8. No ads on the AR camera screen. Preload the interstitial and show it only at the "scan saved" transition, with a frequency cap.
  9. Gallery stored in app-private storage (Room + GLB/JPEG + WebP thumbnails). No media read permissions; export through MediaStore or the share sheet.
  10. CI gates: lint, size budget (bundletool), 16 KB check, R8 r8.json metrics, and a scheduled Macrobenchmark startup run.

1. App size

1.1 Why it matters for India

  • Google's own Play data (2017, still the most-cited figure) shows each extra 6 MB lowers install conversion by about 1 %. In emerging markets, specifically India and Brazil, removing 10 MB raised conversion by about 2.5 % (Google Play blog, "Shrinking APKs, growing installs").
  • Play shows a non-blocking warning to mobile-data users when an app is larger than 200 MB. Current hard limits are: base module 500 MB, each feature module 500 MB, each asset pack 1.5 GB, 4 GB total for modules plus install-time packs, and 30 GB for on-demand/fast-follow packs (Play Console size limits). SpaceView will be nowhere near these limits, so the goal is conversion, not compliance.
  • AR adds a hidden cost. On phones without Google Play Services for AR (the ARCore runtime), the user also downloads roughly 36–38 MB (APKMirror listings for 1.47/1.48, unverified for current builds). With AR Required, Play installs it automatically. With AR Optional, the app must prompt for it (§6.5). The size of our own APK is the only part we control.

1.2 Where the bytes go (expected composition, arm64 split)

Component Rough download contribution Notes
Kotlin/Compose/AndroidX DEX after R8 2–4 MB Compose + Material3 + Navigation + Room + DataStore
play-services-ads (legacy) or GMA Next-Gen 1.5–3 MB (unverified, measure it) Google says Next-Gen is "smaller on device" than the full legacy SDK. The Lite SDK has been deprecated since Jan 2026 (Lite SDK page)
Filament + gltfio native libs (arm64 only) ~3–5 MB The Filament 1.79 AAR is 4.88 MB for all ABIs (Filament releases). Never ship filamat-android (a 12.7 MB AAR with a runtime shader compiler). Use precompiled .filamat materials
ARCore client SDK (com.google.ar:core) < 1 MB The heavy runtime lives in Google Play Services for AR, not in our APK
Materials, IBL/skybox, sample models 0.5–2 MB Use KTX2/compressed IBL, small or no skybox (the camera is the background in AR)
Images, fonts, icons < 0.5 MB Vector drawables or WebP; no custom fonts, or download them later

A 15 MB budget is realistic if we avoid mediation adapters, filamat, Firebase bloat and bundled demo models.

1.3 Build settings (aligned with Gridlark: AGP 9.4.1, Gradle 9.6.1, Kotlin 2.3.21, compileSdk 37, targetSdk 36)

  • Always upload an AAB. Play then serves per-ABI, per-density and per-language splits. Gridlark CI already builds :app:bundleRelease.
  • R8 full mode has been on by default since AGP 8.0. Never add android.enableR8.fullMode=false. AGP 9.0 made optimized resource shrinking the default when isShrinkResources = true, and AGP 9.1 repackages classes by default (Enable app optimization).
  • On AGP 9.3+ you can switch to the new DSL, which turns on code and resource optimisation and includes the default rules (equivalent to proguard-android-optimize.txt):
    android { buildTypes { release { optimization { enable = true } } } }
// keep rules go in src/main/keepRules/*.keep
    Gridlark's legacy form (isMinifyEnabled = true, isShrinkResources = true, proguard-android-optimize.txt) is still supported. Either is fine; pick one and use it in both projects.
  • ABIs: ship arm64-v8a and armeabi-v7a. Some 3 GB Indian phones still run 32-bit userspace, but ARCore certification of those phones is rare, so check the Play device catalogue once the app is live. Add x86_64 only in debug/benchmark builds for emulator and GMD tests. In an AAB, extra ABIs do not change what users download, but they do bloat the universal/test APKs.
    defaultConfig { ndk { abiFilters += listOf("arm64-v8a", "armeabi-v7a") } }
buildTypes { debug { ndk { abiFilters += "x86_64" } } }
  • Native libs uncompressed (useLegacyPackaging = false, the AGP default). This is required for 16 KB alignment and avoids an extracted duplicate on disk. The download stays compressed because Play compresses over the wire.
  • Resources: androidResources { localeFilters += listOf("en", "hi") }. Add more Indian languages later through Play language splits. Strip unused com.google.android.gms locales this way too.
  • Exclude META-INF/*.kotlin_module, *.version, DebugProbesKt.bin and similar in packaging.resources.excludes. Gridlark already excludes AL2.0/LGPL2.1.
  • Do not add mediation in v1. Each adapter brings a full third-party ad SDK (often 2–8 MB each, unverified, so measure before adding), plus more Data-safety rows and more ANR surface.

1.4 Play Feature Delivery / on-demand modules: use sparingly

  • On-demand modules need SplitCompat, network at first use, a UI for "downloading…", and testing with bundletool --local-testing. Feature modules cannot enable minify themselves (the base module controls R8). Exported activities in feature modules are not allowed (Play Feature Delivery).
  • AR is SpaceView's core feature. Putting ARCore and Filament on-demand would make every new user wait through a second download before the first measurement, which is a worse UX than 3–5 MB extra at install. Recommendation: AR/3D rendering stays in the base module in v1.
  • Candidates for on-demand delivery later:
    • an ML segmentation/edge-refinement model (e.g. 3–10 MB TFLite/LiteRT) for better scan cutouts. SceneView's own release notes mention a ~10 MB on-device detector that is "not in the APK" and is downloaded on first use (SceneView releases);
    • a furniture/sample model catalogue.
  • Conditional delivery (<dist:device-feature dist:name="android.hardware.camera.ar"/>) only makes sense if we choose AR Optional and also want the 3D viewer without Filament. We don't, because the gallery's 3D preview needs Filament too.

1.5 Play Asset Delivery (PAD) for 3D assets

  • PAD works for apps as well as games. On-demand packs do not count toward the listed app size; install-time packs do (Play Asset Delivery). Use fast-follow for a small "starter furniture" pack and on-demand for larger catalogues. Assets in packs are read through AssetPackManager file paths, so no extra copy is made.
  • v1 has no catalogue (the gallery is user-generated), so PAD is not needed for v1. Plan for it once more than ~2 MB of bundled models exists.

1.6 Model and texture compression

Asset kind Format Tooling
Bundled/shipped models (samples, UI gizmos) GLB + EXT_meshopt_compression + KHR_mesh_quantization + KTX2 (KHR_texture_basisu): UASTC for normal/ORM maps, ETC1S for base colour gltf-transform optimize in.glb out.glb --compress meshopt --texture-compress ktx2 --texture-size 1024 (needs KTX-Software ≥ 4.4) (glTF Transform)
User-scanned objects (created on the device) GLB containing a box or low-poly mesh (tens to hundreds of vertices, so no mesh compression needed) and one JPEG texture atlas (1024×1024, q≈80, ~150–350 KB) Encode on the device with Bitmap.compress(JPEG). Do not encode KTX2/Basis on the device: it is slow, CPU-heavy and needs an extra native encoder library
Thumbnails WebP (lossy, q≈75, 256–384 px longest side, ~10–25 KB) Bitmap.compress(WEBP_LOSSY)
UI images Vector drawables or WebP AGP/aapt
  • Filament gltfio supports KHR_texture_basisu, EXT_meshopt_compression, KHR_mesh_quantization, KHR_draco_mesh_compression and KHR_texture_transform (Filament README). Prefer meshopt over Draco because it decodes faster and needs a smaller decoder. Draco support is described as "platform-dependent".
  • gltfio does not list EXT_texture_webp, so textures inside GLBs must be JPEG/PNG (or KTX2). WebP is for thumbnails only.
  • Check that the chosen SceneView version passes a KTX2 provider through to gltfio. This is a question for the rendering agent.

Do: AAB only; R8 full + resource shrink; arm64 + v7a; filamat banned; no mediation; meshopt + KTX2 for shipped assets; JPEG atlas in GLB for user scans; a size budget enforced in CI (§7.5).

2. Startup and runtime performance

2.1 Baseline and Startup Profiles without Android Studio

  • Baseline Profiles precompile the hot paths. Startup Profiles also reorder DEX so startup code sits in classes.dex, which is "usually 15–30 % faster than Baseline Profiles alone". They need R8 enabled and AGP ≥ 8.3 for DEX layout optimisation (on by default) (Startup Profiles).
  • Generate them entirely from Gradle with a Gradle Managed Device. The image must be AOSP because the generator needs root (Configure Baseline Profile generation):
    // :baselineprofile (com.android.test + androidx.baselineprofile plugin)
android {
  testOptions.managedDevices.allDevices {
    create<com.android.build.api.dsl.ManagedVirtualDevice>("pixel6Api34") {
      device = "Pixel 6"; apiLevel = 34; systemImageSource = "aosp"
    }
  }
}
baselineProfile { managedDevices += "pixel6Api34"; useConnectedDevices = false }
// :app
baselineProfile { saveInSrc = true; mergeIntoMain = true }   // commit the generated profile
    Run ./gradlew :app:generateBaselineProfile (or generateReleaseBaselineProfile). Output goes to src/main/generated/baselineProfiles/{baseline-prof.txt,startup-prof.txt}. In the generator, call rule.collect(packageName, includeInStartupProfile = true) { … } for the launch journey.
  • CI plan: a separate GitHub Actions workflow (workflow_dispatch plus weekly cron, or on v* tags) on ubuntu-latest with KVM enabled through the usual udev rule. GitHub's Linux runners support hardware-accelerated emulators (android-emulator-runner README). The workflow regenerates profiles and opens a PR with the diff. Do not use automaticGenerationDuringBuild = true in the normal build, because it doubles build time.
  • Journeys to profile: cold launch → home; home → gallery grid scroll; gallery → 3D viewer. AR camera screens cannot run on an emulator (an ARCore emulator needs a special virtual scene), so profile up to "AR screen composed", not tracking itself.
  • The compiled profile must be under 1.5 MB. Keep journeys focused.
  • Gridlark already depends on androidx.profileinstaller. SpaceView should do the same, so sideloaded/internal builds also get the profile.

2.2 Startup architecture

  • Application.onCreate should be close to empty. Lazy-initialise everything: Room DB on first use, AdMob after consent on a background thread (§5), ARCore availability check deferred (below), Filament Engine created only on entering a 3D/AR screen.
  • Use the App Startup library instead of content providers for any SDK that insists on auto-init. Remove auto-init providers we don't need with tools:node="remove" (e.g. WorkManager initialiser if unused) (Launch time guide).
  • Use the SplashScreen API (core-splashscreen, as in Gridlark). No custom splash activity.
  • ARCore availability: Google recommends calling ArCoreApk.checkAvailabilityAsync() early and ignoring the result so a cached answer is ready when the AR button is shown (Enable ARCore). Do this after the first frame (Choreographer/LaunchedEffect on the home screen), not in Application.
  • Report TTFD with ReportDrawnWhen { galleryLoaded } so Macrobenchmark and vitals measure the real "usable" time.
  • Targets: cold TTID ≤ 800 ms on mid-range, ≤ 1.5 s on 3 GB phones. Vitals thresholds are cold ≥ 5 s, warm ≥ 2 s and hot ≥ 1.5 s (vitals launch time).

2.3 Compose performance

  • Strong skipping is on by default since Kotlin 2.0.20, so we're covered. Unstable parameters can still skip by instance equality, and lambdas are auto-remembered (Strong skipping). We still want:
    • @Immutable/@Stable on UI models, or kotlinx.collections.immutable for lists;
    • a stabilityConfigurationFiles entry for domain types from non-Compose modules (e.g. java.time.*, our Dimensions value class);
    • Compose compiler reports behind a Gradle property, checked occasionally.
  • Lazy grids for the gallery: key = { it.id } and contentType. Thumbnails load through Coil 3 with explicit size(), which avoids decoding full-size images.
  • Defer state reads: use lambda modifiers (Modifier.offset { }, graphicsLayer { }, drawBehind) for anything that animates per frame.
  • The AR overlay is the main jank risk. Do not push per-frame AR data (pose, distance, tracking state) into Compose state at 30–60 Hz:
    • draw reticles and measurement lines in the 3D renderer, not in Compose;
    • expose a StateFlow throttled to about 10 Hz with distinctUntilChanged() (e.g. distance rounded to 1 mm) for labels, collected with collectAsStateWithLifecycle();
    • use snapshotFlow/conflate() for any reverse direction.
  • Always benchmark release-like builds (benchmarkRelease). Debug Compose is much slower.

2.4 Threading and the AR frame loop

  • ARCore's session.update() and rendering run on the GL/render thread (SceneView manages this). That thread must never do disk or network I/O, Bitmap compression, Room queries or JSON parsing.
  • Do heavy work off the render and main threads:
    • YUV→RGB conversion, texture atlas building, JPEG/WebP encode, GLB writing: use Dispatchers.Default.limitedParallelism(2). Limiting parallelism stops scan processing from starving ARCore's VIO tracking threads. ARCore logs "VIO frequency low" when CPU starvation hurts tracking (ARCore performance);
    • Room and file I/O: use Dispatchers.IO.
  • CPU camera images: the default CPU stream is 640×480 and costs nothing extra. Any higher resolution adds a second stream, which costs real CPU/memory (ARCore ML guide). For photo textures, capture one higher-resolution frame only when the user presses "Capture". Don't stream it.
  • Always close camera images: frame.acquireCameraImage().use { … }. Treat NotYetAvailableException as "try the next frame". Holding images causes ResourceExhaustedException. Copy the planes out quickly and process the copy on a worker thread.
  • Use CameraConfigFilter.setTargetFps(EnumSet.of(TARGET_FPS_30)) and setDepthSensorUsage(DO_NOT_USE) unless depth is needed. 60 fps "increases power consumption and memory usage and is not supported on all devices", and 30 fps is available on every ARCore device (CameraConfig.TargetFps).

2.5 GPU and rendering budget (renderer-agnostic, Filament-centric)

  • Render at camera rate (30 fps). Don't render extra frames when nothing changes in the non-AR 3D viewer: render on demand, or pause the Choreographer callback when idle.
  • Low tier (≤ 4 GB RAM or Mali-G52-class GPU): no SSAO, no bloom, FXAA or no AA (no MSAA), one directional light with a ≤ 1024 shadow map (or a fake contact-shadow blob), dynamic resolution allowed down to 0.75.
  • Light estimation: AMBIENT_INTENSITY on the low tier; ENVIRONMENTAL_HDR only on the high tier, because HDR cubemap estimation costs CPU/GPU.
  • Plane finding: HORIZONTAL (add vertical only for wall measuring). Disable plane finding once an object is placed. Disable Instant Placement once full tracking exists. ARCore lists both as CPU-heavy (ARCore performance).
  • Depth API (over 88 % of active devices support it, per Google as of May 2026): useful for measurement on non-planar surfaces and for occlusion, but expensive. Make it opt-in or automatic only on the high tier, and turn it off under thermal pressure.
  • Choose the tier with ActivityManager.isLowRamDevice(), MemoryInfo.totalMem, memoryClass and the GPU renderer string, all cached in DataStore on first launch.

2.6 Memory on low-RAM phones

  • Vitals now has memory core vitals: Anon RSS + swap P99 per RAM tier (4 GB tier, foreground: 2.0 GB) and bitmap memory (200 MB). Play may reduce visibility for apps over these thresholds from February 2027 (Android vitals; memory usage). Phones below 4 GB have no threshold, but they are where low-memory kills happen. Watch the LMK vital.
  • Budget for an AR session on a 3–4 GB phone: ≤ 500–700 MB. The main consumers:
    • camera buffers (owned by ARCore);
    • Filament: an engine with ~5–20 MB of buffers plus textures. Each 1024² texture with mips ≈ 5.3 MB of GPU memory; a 2048² one ≈ 21 MB. Cap user textures at 1024² on the low tier and limit simultaneous placed objects (e.g. 6 on the low tier);
    • an AdMob banner WebView (tens of MB; destroy, not just hide, banners before entering AR).
  • Respond to onTrimMemory(TRIM_MEMORY_UI_HIDDEN / BACKGROUND): release the Filament scene and textures and clear the Coil memory cache.
  • Filament objects are native and not garbage-collected. Every Texture, MaterialInstance, Entity, IndirectLight and Engine must be destroyed explicitly. LeakCanary won't see these leaks, so check with adb shell dumpsys meminfo <pkg> before and after 10 cycles of entering and leaving the 3D screen.
  • ARCore session lifecycle: create once per AR screen; pause() in onPause (frees the camera); close() when the screen is destroyed. Avoid fast create/destroy loops; older ARCore versions leaked memory on repeated session creation (EasyAR notes for ARCore 1.13, unverified for current versions).
  • Don't retain Activity, View or Session in ViewModels. Hold AR state in a screen-scoped holder that's disposed through DisposableEffect/lifecycle observers.

Do: GMD-generated Baseline and Startup profiles; empty Application.onCreate; throttled AR→Compose state; 30 fps, depth off, plane finding off after placement; device tiering; explicit Filament destruction; close every Image.

3. Storage design (AR Gallery)

3.1 Layout (app-private, so no storage permissions)

filesDir/gallery/<uuid>/model.glb      // box/low-poly mesh + 1024² JPEG atlas, 150–500 KB
filesDir/gallery/<uuid>/thumb.webp     // 384 px, ~15–25 KB
filesDir/gallery/<uuid>/source.jpg     // OPTIONAL original capture, off by default (saves space)
cacheDir/capture/…                      // temp frames, deleted after the save completes
  • Room (as in Gridlark: Room 2.8.x + KSP, exported schemas) holds metadata: id, name, width/height/depth in mm (store integers in millimetres, never floats in cm), created/modified, file sizes, thumbnail path, schema version, measurement accuracy/confidence.
  • DataStore (Preferences) holds settings: units, device tier cache, consent-derived flags, ad frequency counters.
  • Writes are atomic. Write to model.glb.tmp, fsync, then rename, then insert the Room row in a transaction. On startup, a sweep deletes orphan directories and rows whose files are missing.
  • Scoped storage: no READ_MEDIA_IMAGES or WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE. Play's Photo & Video policy allows broad media permissions only for apps whose core purpose is broad media access. Everyone else must use the Photo Picker (Photo & Video Permissions policy). To import a photo, use PickVisualMedia. To export, use MediaStore inserts into Pictures/SpaceView (no permission needed on API 29+) and FileProvider + share sheet for .glb.
  • Before saving: check StorageManager.getAllocatableBytes() and show a clear "storage full" message. Many Indian low-end phones run close to full.
  • Cache eviction: cacheDir/capture is cleared after every scan and on launch; Coil disk cache ≤ 50 MB; never put gallery data in cacheDir, because the OS may delete it.
  • Backup: Auto Backup is capped at 25 MB per app. Either exclude gallery/ and the Room DB together in dataExtractionRules (so no rows point at missing files), or include both and accept partial restore. Recommended for v1: exclude both, and offer an explicit "Export all" (.zip) later.
  • Image pipeline: capture frame → crop/rectify each face on a worker thread → downscale with Bitmap.createScaledBitmap or inSampleSize to ≤ 1024 px per face/atlas → recycle intermediates → JPEG q80 into the GLB. Display thumbnails with Coil (size(256)). Use Bitmap.Config.HARDWARE for read-only display bitmaps to keep them out of the Java heap.
  • Approximate storage per scan: 0.2–0.5 MB, so 100 scans ≈ 20–50 MB. Show total gallery size in Settings.

Do: private storage, Room in millimetres, atomic writes, no media permissions, a consistent backup policy, an allocatable-bytes check.

4. Battery and thermal behaviour in long AR sessions

  • AR keeps the camera, VIO/SLAM, GPU and display busy all at once. On low-end SoCs, a session can reach THERMAL_STATUS_MODERATE within minutes.
  • Monitoring (ARCore performance; ADPF thermal):
    • PowerManager.addThermalStatusListener (API 29+) and getThermalHeadroom(forecastSeconds) (API 30+). Call headroom at most once every 10 s (more often returns NaN). A NaN or 0 first value means the API isn't supported.
    • Step-down ladder: headroom ≥ 0.85 or MODERATE → disable Depth and HDR light estimation, dynamic resolution to 0.75, shadows off. Headroom ≥ 0.95 or SEVERE → pause the AR session and show "Phone is warm, AR paused". CRITICAL → end the session.
  • Idle auto-pause: if there's no touch and the reticle hasn't moved for about 60 s, pause the session and show "Tap to resume". That saves battery, and the camera LED/indicator turns off.
  • Use FLAG_KEEP_SCREEN_ON on the AR window only. Never use partial wake locks. "Excessive partial wake locks" is a core vital with a 5 % threshold (Android vitals).
  • No background work during or after AR. No WorkManager jobs are needed in v1.
  • Set the display refresh rate preference for the AR screen to 60 Hz (preferredDisplayModeId/Surface.setFrameRate(30f, …) on the render surface). This stops 120 Hz panels burning power on a 30 fps camera feed.
  • Test protocol: a 15-minute continuous session on a 4 GB MediaTek Helio G-series phone and a Snapdragon 6-series phone. Log thermal status, fps, battery % and dropped frames, and record a Perfetto trace. Google's ADPF guidance recommends sessions of at least 15 minutes to see thermal stabilisation.

5. AdMob performance and compliance

5.1 SDK choice

  • Legacy play-services-ads 25.x (Gridlark uses 25.5.0 + UMP 4.0.0): mature and supports all mediation. Since 24.0.0, OPTIMIZE_INITIALIZATION and OPTIMIZE_AD_LOADING are on by default (Optimize initialization).
  • GMA Next-Gen SDK (com.google.android.libraries.ads.mobile.sdk:ads-mobile-sdk, 1.6.0 on the quick-start page):
    • minSdk 24;
    • smaller on device than the legacy SDK (per Google);
    • built-in preloading API: call start once, then pollAd(); the SDK refills and retries automatically; at most 6 preloaded ads in total; a buffer of 2 per preload ID is recommended (Next-Gen ad preloading);
    • callbacks arrive on a background thread, so UI work must be posted to the main thread;
    • mediation only works through AdMob mediation.
    • Third-party posts say it reached 1.x in April 2026 and became Google's "preferred" Android SDK in July 2026 (unverified).
  • Recommendation: choose Next-Gen for SpaceView if the official page shows it as GA when implementation starts. We have no mediation in v1, size matters, and preloading fits the "interstitial after scan" pattern. Otherwise use legacy 25.x to match Gridlark. Either way, keep the ads behind a small AdsGateway interface so they can be swapped.

5.2 Initialisation order (no main-thread blocking)

  1. First frame drawn (home screen).
  2. Call UMP requestConsentInfoUpdate on every launch. Show the form if needed. canRequestAds() returns false until the update has been called (UMP privacy).
  3. When canRequestAds() is true, call MobileAds.initialize() on a background thread (Dispatchers.IO), once per process. The Next-Gen docs warn that initialising on the main thread may cause an ANR (Next-Gen quick start).
  4. If there's no mediation, MobileAds.disableMediationAdapterInitialization() (legacy) skips adapter start-up work.
  5. Start preloading the interstitial when the user enters the scan flow, not at app start. That saves data and memory on users who only browse.

5.3 Placement rules (performance + policy)

  • No ads on the AR camera screen, neither banner nor interstitial, during tracking:
    • a banner WebView costs memory and GPU composition on top of the camera surface;
    • it invites accidental clicks;
    • it competes with VIO for CPU. Destroy the banner AdView when navigating into AR and recreate it afterwards.
  • Interstitial "per scan" is shown at the transition after "Saved to gallery", never in the middle of an AR session and never on app open/exit. AdMob explicitly disallows interstitials on app load or exit, after every user action, immediately after another interstitial, or ones that launch unexpectedly (AdMob interstitial guidance). A completed scan is a reasonable natural break.
    • Add a frequency cap anyway: first scan of the session ad-free, then at most one per 3 minutes and at most one per 2 scans. This protects ratings and AdMob account health.
  • Only show a preloaded ad. If none is ready, skip it. Never show a spinner while waiting for an ad.
  • Adaptive anchored banners go only on gallery and list screens. Pause them in onPause.
  • Mute video ads (MobileAds.setAppMuted(true) / Next-Gen equivalent) to save bandwidth and avoid annoyance.
  • Data use: each banner refresh costs mobile data, so use the default 60 s refresh or longer, not 30 s.

5.4 Consent and privacy for India

  • UMP messages are legally required for the EEA/UK/Switzerland (GDPR) and US state-privacy regimes. India is not a UMP-message region. Still ship UMP: it's already in Gridlark, Play distribution may extend beyond India, and it provides the privacy-options entry point.
  • DPDP Act 2023 / DPDP Rules 2025:
    • the Rules were notified on 14 Nov 2025 with phased commencement;
    • notice, consent, security safeguards, breach reporting, data principal rights and children's data rules apply 18 months later (~13–14 May 2027; law-firm sources differ by a day) (PIB/MeitY doc; The Week).
    • Implications for planning:
      1. Keep all scan images and measurements on the device (not collected). This makes SpaceView a very small data fiduciary.
      2. Publish a clear privacy notice in English and Hindi before launch.
      3. The DPDP Act treats anyone under 18 as a child and bars tracking, behavioural monitoring and targeted advertising directed at children. Set the Play target audience to 18+ (or 13+ without child-directed content), and plan for non-personalised ads (npa/Limited Ads, or a "tag for under age of consent") if child users are possible. Legal review needed before May 2027.
  • Advertising ID: play-services-ads merges com.google.android.gms.permission.AD_ID. Complete the Play Console Advertising ID declaration (required for target 33+).

Do: Next-Gen (if GA) or legacy 25.x; init after consent, off the main thread; preload at scan-flow entry; no ads in AR; frequency cap; no mediation in v1; DPDP-ready privacy notice by May 2027.

6. Play Store requirements (as of November 2026)

6.1 Target API level

  • New apps and updates must target Android 16 (API 36) from 31 Aug 2026. Existing apps must target ≥ 35 to stay visible to new users on newer Android versions (Target API requirements). Expect API 37 to be required around Aug 2027. compileSdk 37 is already in Gridlark and is required by SceneView 4.65.
  • Behaviour changes to plan for at target 36:
    • Edge-to-edge is enforced (since target 35). Use Compose WindowInsets and place AR controls in safe areas.
    • Orientation and resizability: on large screens (smallest width ≥ 600 dp), target 36 ignores screenOrientation, resizeableActivity and min/max aspect ratio. An opt-out exists at API 36 only; there is no opt-out at API 37 (Android 16 orientation changes).
      • The AR screen must therefore handle rotation and resizing on tablets and foldables. Update ARCore display geometry on configuration change and avoid recreating the session on rotation (configChanges with a carefully handled onConfigurationChanged, or a ViewModel-held session).
      • Phones under 600 dp can still lock portrait.
    • Predictive back is enabled by default for target 36. Make sure the AR screen's back handling (e.g. "discard measurement?") uses PredictiveBackHandler/BackHandler.

6.2 16 KB memory page size (critical for ARCore + Filament)

  • Rule: apps targeting Android 15+ must support 16 KB pages on 64-bit devices. From 1 Feb 2027, Play blocks updates that don't (16 KB page sizes guide). Without it, the app fails or runs in "page size compat mode" with a warning dialog on 16 KB devices (Pixel 8+ dev option, newer OEM phones).
  • Requirements:
    • AGP ≥ 8.5.1 (we have 9.4) with uncompressed native libs, which zip-aligns them to 16 KB;
    • every .so with LOAD segments aligned to 2**14 and RELRO correct;
    • NDK r28+ for anything we compile ourselves (16 KB by default).
  • ARCore: Unity's ARCore plugin changelog says ARCore 1.48 fixed 16 KB alignment for its binaries (inferred, unverified from Google's own notes). Current is 1.57.0 (16 Oct 2026), which targets API 37 (ARCore SDK releases). Use the latest.
  • Filament / SceneView: Filament's release notes don't mention 16 KB, but the flutter_sceneview 4.24 changelog lists "Google Play 16 KB page-size alignment for Filament native libs" fixes (flutter_sceneview changelog). The latest is SceneView 4.65.0 (1 Nov 2026), with Filament 1.79.0 (26 Oct 2026). Verify in CI. Don't trust the changelogs alone.
  • CI check (Linux runner, no Studio needed):
    $ANDROID_HOME/build-tools/36.0.0/zipalign -c -P 16 -v 4 app-release.apk   # "Verification successful"
# plus, for every lib/arm64-v8a/*.so and lib/x86_64/*.so:
llvm-objdump -p lib.so | grep LOAD        # every 'align 2**14' (or higher)
# or simply: check_elf_alignment.sh app-release.apk  (AOSP script) -> all ALIGNED
bundletool dump config --bundle=app.aab | grep -i alignment   # expect PAGE_ALIGNMENT_16K
    Also run the app on a 16 KB emulator image in the GMD job (Google APIs Experimental 16 KB Page Size x86_64 system image).

6.3 Data safety form (planned answers; confirm at submission)

Data Collected/shared by Purpose
IP / approximate location, app interactions, diagnostics, device or other IDs (Advertising ID, app set ID) AdMob: collected and shared, encrypted in transit Advertising, analytics, fraud prevention (AdMob data disclosure)
Crash logs, diagnostics Crashlytics/Sentry, if added App functionality / analytics
Photos and camera frames, measurements, 3D models Not collected. Processed and stored only on the device, never sent off it —
  • Mark "data can be deleted" (uninstall / clear the gallery). Link a privacy policy URL (Gridlark already wires PRIVACY_POLICY_URL from CI secrets; reuse that pattern).

6.4 Camera permission

  • Request CAMERA at runtime only when the user taps Measure or Scan, with a short pre-prompt explaining why. Don't request it at launch. Handle "Don't ask again" with a Settings deep link. Permission denials are a vital (vitals list).
  • No microphone, location or media permissions. Keep the manifest minimal and check it in CI by comparing the merged manifest's permission list.

6.5 AR Required vs AR Optional

AR Required AR Optional
Manifest <uses-feature android:name="android.hardware.camera.ar"/> + <meta-data android:name="com.google.ar.core" android:value="required"/> <meta-data … android:value="optional"/>, no camera.ar feature
Play visibility Only ARCore-certified devices All compatible devices
Installs Google Play Services for AR Automatically, with the app No. The app must call requestInstall() when AR is first used
minSdk 24 24 for AR features
Source: Enable ARCore.

Recommendation: AR Required for v1.

  • Every core feature (measure, scan, place) needs ARCore. On a non-certified phone, an AR Optional install could only browse an empty gallery. That produces 1-star reviews, refunds of attention, and uninstalls, which hurt Play ranking.
  • AR Required also removes the in-app "install ARCore" step (one less failure point and one less ANR risk).
  • Cost: reach is limited to ARCore-certified models. Many sub-₹10k phones are not certified (download the device CSV from the Play Console device catalogue and check top Indian models, such as Redmi, Realme, Samsung A/M and Moto G series, before launch).
  • Revisit later: switch to AR Optional if a non-AR feature is added, such as manual photo-based measurement or a pure 3D gallery.
  • Even with AR Required, still call checkAvailability and requestInstall before creating the session, because users can sideload or have an outdated ARCore. Handle UnavailableUserDeclinedInstallationException cleanly.

6.6 Android vitals (what Play enforces)

  • Core vitals:
    • user-perceived crash rate: 1.09 % overall, 8 % per phone model;
    • user-perceived ANR rate: 0.47 % overall, 8 % per phone model;
    • excessive partial wake locks: 5 %;
    • memory: Anon RSS + swap P99 per RAM tier, and bitmap memory. Memory and DEX code optimisation thresholds may affect visibility from Feb 2027. The DEX vital applies only to apps with > 10 MB DEX, which we won't reach, but enabling R8 covers it anyway (Android vitals; DEX code optimisation).
  • Play uses a 28-day rolling window. Set up email alerts and use the Play Developer Reporting API if we automate dashboards.
  • AR-specific crash and ANR risks:
    • GPU driver crashes in Filament on specific Mali/PowerVR devices: watch per-device rates and exclude broken models in the device catalogue if needed;
    • ANRs from synchronous ARCore install checks or session creation on the main thread: create the session off the main thread where the API allows, and never block the UI on checkAvailability;
    • the main thread stalling on GLB writes.

6.7 Pre-launch reports, testing tracks, Play Integrity, in-app review/updates

  • New personal developer accounts (created after 13 Nov 2023) must run a closed test with ≥ 12 testers opted in for 14 continuous days before production access. This is per new app (Play Console help). Build this into the timeline. Organisation (D-U-N-S) accounts are exempt.
  • Pre-launch reports run automatically on uploads to testing tracks (Robo crawler on real devices). The AR screen will only show a camera feed. Provide a Robo script or test account path that exercises gallery, settings and the 3D viewer, and treat its crash, ANR, accessibility and security findings as release blockers.
  • Play Integrity: not needed for v1. There's no server, no paid content, and nothing worth protecting. If added later, use the standard request with prepareIntegrityToken at a quiet moment, not on the startup path; preparation is limited to 5 times per minute per app instance (Standard requests).
  • In-app review (review-ktx, as in Gridlark): ask after the user's third successful saved scan, never from a button. Quota is enforced and the dialog may not show (In-App Review).
  • In-app updates: use the flexible flow for normal releases and immediate only for critical fixes. Check at most once per day, after first frame, never while in AR.

7. Quality tooling

7.1 R8 keep rules

  • First rely on consumer rules shipped in the AARs: ARCore, play-services-ads, Room, Compose and Coil all ship them. Add our own rules only after a release-build crash names the missing class.
  • Filament/gltfio/SceneView:
    • JNI entry points are kept by Filament's native-method rules; check whether the SceneView AAR ships consumer-rules.pro (unverified; ask the rendering agent to check).
    • If R8 breaks JNI callbacks, keep only the classes/methods called from native code (e.g. -keepclasseswithmembernames class * { native <methods>; } is already in the default rules).
    • Avoid -keep class com.google.android.filament.** { *; }. Broad package keeps stop most optimisation, and they show up in the R8 Configuration Analyzer (R8 keep rules blog).
  • kotlinx.serialization (if used for metadata) needs the official rules, which the plugin now ships.
  • Keep the mapping.txt upload in CI (Gridlark already archives it). Upload it to Play automatically with the release, so vitals stack traces are deobfuscated. Upload native debug symbols where available.
  • Use the R8 Configuration Analyzer (AGP ≥ 9.3) and inspect BUNDLE-METADATA/com.android.tools/r8.json from the AAB to track the optimisation, obfuscation and shrinking percentages Play sees (DEX code optimisation).
  • Test the release build on a real device every sprint. R8 bugs only appear in release builds.

7.2 LeakCanary and StrictMode (debug only)

  • LeakCanary: debugImplementation("com.squareup.leakcanary:leakcanary-android:2.14") (stable). 3.0 is still alpha (3.0-alpha-9), so don't use it. LeakCanary catches Activity, Fragment, ViewModel and View leaks. It won't catch Filament or ARCore native leaks (see §2.6 meminfo protocol).
  • StrictMode in debug Application: ThreadPolicy.detectDiskReads/Writes/Network().penaltyLog() and VmPolicy.detectLeakedClosableObjects().detectActivityLeaks().detectLeakedRegistrationObjects().detectUnsafeIntentLaunch(). Expect some noise from the AdMob and ARCore SDKs; allowlist them by stack.
  • debugImplementation only. Nothing debug-only may reach release (lint and the dependency check enforce this).

7.3 Crash reporting: Crashlytics vs lighter options

Option Size Pros Cons
Play Console Android vitals (built in) 0 Free; counts crashes before SDK init; native crashes with uploaded symbols; this is the metric Play actually ranks on Only Play installs from users who opted in to diagnostics; few breadcrumbs; ~1-day delay
Firebase Crashlytics Small core (Google claims ~40 KB, unverified) plus Firebase common/transport deps; NDK variant adds .so Custom keys (GPU, ARCore version, tier), breadcrumbs, non-fatals, near-real-time, free Adds the Google Services plugin/google-services.json; more Data-safety rows; init cost at startup
Sentry ~1 MB (PayU measurement, unverified/dated) Rich, self-hostable Heavier; paid at scale
ACRA Small Self-hosted, no Google dependency You run a backend; limited UI

Recommendation:

  • Launch with Play vitals plus ApplicationExitInfo (API 30+, logged locally and shown in a debug screen). This adds zero size and no Data-safety change.
  • Add Crashlytics (non-NDK) in the first update if vitals shows device-specific GPU/AR crashes we can't diagnose. Its custom keys (GPU renderer string, ARCore APK version, device tier) are the main reason to adopt it.
  • Gridlark has no crash SDK either, so this keeps the two projects consistent.

7.4 Profiling without Android Studio

  • Perfetto: adb shell perfetto with the atrace/gfx/view/camera/power categories, or the on-device System Tracing app. Open traces at ui.perfetto.dev. Look for Choreographer#doFrame, Compose:recompose and the RenderThread/GL thread slices (launch-time guide).
  • Startup: adb shell am start -S -W <pkg>/.MainActivity gives TotalTime (TTID) quickly. Use Macrobenchmark StartupTimingMetric/FrameTimingMetric for repeatable numbers.
  • Memory: adb shell dumpsys meminfo <pkg>; dumpsys gfxinfo <pkg> framestats.
  • ARCore: adb logcat | grep "VIO frequency low".
  • Android also now ships an Android CLI with agent "skills" (e.g. android skills add r8-analyzer) that runs without the IDE (Enable app optimization). Worth evaluating for this IDE-less workflow.

7.5 CI checks (extending Gridlark's android.yml)

Gridlark's workflow already runs tests, lintDebug (abortOnError = true), assembleDebug, release APK+AAB, and archives the mapping. Add these for SpaceView:

  1. :app:lintRelease, which includes the 16 KB alignment lint check for native libraries.
  2. Size budget:
    bundletool build-apks --bundle=app-release.aab --output=app.apks --device-spec=ci/arm64-xxhdpi-api30-en.json
bundletool get-size total --apks=app.apks --device-spec=ci/arm64-xxhdpi-api30-en.json   # fail if MAX > 18 MB
    Also write get-size total --dimensions=ABI,SCREEN_DENSITY into the job summary to track trends (bundletool).
  3. 16 KB check: zipalign -P 16 plus check_elf_alignment.sh on the release APK, as in §6.2.
  4. Native library inventory: list lib/arm64-v8a/*.so with sizes and fail on unexpected additions (e.g. libfilamat*, mediation SDKs).
  5. Dependency guard: ./gradlew :app:dependencies --configuration releaseRuntimeClasspath > deps.txt, diffed against a committed baseline (or the dependency-guard plugin). Fail on unreviewed changes.
  6. Merged-manifest permission check: compare the permissions in build/intermediates/merged_manifests/release/.../AndroidManifest.xml against an allowlist (CAMERA, INTERNET, ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE, AD_ID).
  7. R8 metrics: extract r8.json from the AAB and store it as an artifact.
  8. Scheduled perf job (weekly + on v* tags): a GMD (AOSP, API 34, KVM) runs the Baseline Profile generator and a Macrobenchmark cold-start run. Store the median and P90. Alert when the median regresses by more than 10 % compared with the last run (absolute emulator numbers are noisy).
  9. Keep REQUIRE_RELEASE_CONFIG gating from Gridlark (no test ads, no debug signing on tags).

Toolchain alignment note: the SceneView README (v4.65.0) lists AGP 9.4.0+, Kotlin 2.4, JVM target 21, compileSdk 37, minSdk 24 (SceneView README). Gridlark is on Kotlin 2.3.21 with Java 17 bytecode. If SceneView is chosen, SpaceView needs Kotlin 2.4.x (plus a matching KSP and Compose compiler plugin) and jvmTarget = 21. JDK 21 on CI already supports this. Pass this to the rendering agent.

8. What "top-notch" looks like (benchmarks to hold ourselves to)

  • Download size: best-in-class utility apps in India stay under ~15 MB, and "Lite" versions of major apps target ≤ 10 MB. A Filament + ARCore app at ≤ 15 MB would be excellent. 15–25 MB is acceptable. Above 30 MB measurably hurts conversion in India (§1.1).
  • Cold start:
    • well-tuned Compose apps with Baseline and Startup profiles reach about 400–700 ms TTID on mid-range phones (industry rule of thumb, not an official figure);
    • Startup Profiles are worth a further 15–30 % (official);
    • Play's floor is 5 s cold, 2 s warm and 1.5 s hot.
    • Our targets: ≤ 800 ms mid-range, ≤ 1.5 s on 3 GB phones.
  • Stability: top apps sit around 0.1–0.3 % crash rate and below 0.1–0.2 % ANR rate, versus Play's bad-behaviour lines of 1.09 % and 0.47 %. Our targets: crash < 0.3 % and ANR < 0.15 % overall, and no single phone model above 2 %.
  • Rendering: a steady 30 fps in AR (matching the camera) is better than a variable 30–60. Google's ARCore guidance says users "prefer frame rates that are consistent and lower" (ARCore performance). Main-thread frames < 16 ms in the gallery (Macrobenchmark FrameTimingMetric P95 < 12 ms).
  • Memory: P99 foreground well under the 2.0 GB 4 GB-tier limit (aim ≤ 700 MB). P99/P50 ratio < 3.5×; Google flags ≥ 3.5× as a likely leak (memory usage vital).

9. Open questions for other agents / the developer

  1. Rendering agent:
    • confirm the SceneView/Filament versions are 16 KB-aligned (run the CI check on a hello-world AAR);
    • confirm SceneView does not pull in filamat-android;
    • confirm it supports a KTX2 provider and meshopt;
    • check whether it ships R8 consumer rules.
  2. Product: confirm AR Required at launch (§6.5) and the interstitial frequency cap (§5.3).
  3. Legal: DPDP children's data and targeted ads (target audience setting) before May 2027.
  4. Play account type: personal (needs the 12-tester, 14-day closed test) or organisation.
  5. Ads SDK: Next-Gen vs legacy, decided by Next-Gen's official GA status at implementation time.

Researchdocs/research/pro-monetization.md · updated 05 Nov 2026, 21:29

SpaceView: Pro Tier, Pricing and Profitability

Research date: 5 November 2026. M3 is done; M4 (place in room) is next. This document builds on business.md (2 Nov 2026) and ui-ux.md §5 and supersedes their Pro pricing where they differ. Prepared for a solo founder publishing from a personal Play developer account in India.

How to read this. Figures marked [est.] are my working assumptions, not sourced data. USD to INR uses about ₹88/$. "Net" means what reaches the bank after Google Play's service fee and GST (18%, included in the Play price), unless it says "pre-GST". docs/research/3d-capture.md (the cloud-scan cost study) had not landed at the time of writing, so cloud-scan costs are shown at ₹5, ₹15 (base) and ₹40 per scan. Section 3.4 gives the rule that converts the real cost into allowances once that report lands.

0. Executive summary

  1. Never paywall accuracy. Measure, ±, the quality badge, calibration, history, object scan, place-in-room and the Fits / Tight / Too big verdict all stay free and unlimited. They earn the reviews and feed the B2B story. Pro sells output and convenience: no ads, unlimited gallery, PDF / hi-res / 3D exports, the full furniture template library, compare, unlimited on-device 3D, cloud backup, and cloud-scan credits.

  2. Price slightly above the old plan, because the product now does more and GST plus Play take about 30%:

    • Pro Monthly ₹129
    • Pro Yearly ₹799 (≈ ₹67/mo, 7-day free trial)
    • Pro 30-day Pass ₹149: prepaid, does not auto-renew. This fits India's "I'm moving / buying a sofa this month" job.
    • Lifetime ₹1,999 later (v1.x). It never includes cloud credits.
    • These prices are still below Spotify Premium India (₹139/mo) and far below the IN/SEA subscription-app medians ($3.75/mo, $18.32/yr). business.md's ₹99 / ₹599 becomes the low arm of a price test.

  3. Cloud 3D scans must be metered with credits, never "unlimited". At ₹40 per scan, a heavy Yearly user who uses three scans a month loses money. Included credits are therefore granted monthly and capped, with a capped rollover. Top-up packs are priced at ≥ 2× the per-scan cost. Base case (₹15/scan):

    • Monthly plan: 3 credits/month. Yearly plan: 2 credits/month. Pass: 3 credits.
    • Packs: 3 for ₹99 and 10 for ₹299.
    • Free users get no free cloud scans; they can buy packs.

  4. Unit economics (base, ₹15/scan, with GST):

    • Net revenue per payer per month: Monthly ₹90, Yearly ₹46.
    • Gross margin per payer at light / typical / heavy use: Monthly 97% / 80% / 47%, Yearly 94% / 61% / 29%. Heavy use stays positive at every cost point because of the allowance table in §3.4.
    • Expected blended ARPPU is about ₹82 gross / ₹57 net per month.

  5. Expect low conversion. RevenueCat's 2026 median download-to-paid within 35 days for IN/SEA users is 0.7%, against 2.8% in North America; the IN/SEA top quartile is 1.9% [1]. The base case uses 0.8%.

    • Base 12-month net contribution is about ₹4.4 lakh, with month-12 run-rate about ₹84k/mo, of which about ₹40k is B2B.
    • Conservative is about ₹0.7 lakh; optimistic about ₹18 lakh.
    • The backend breaks even at about 70–180 paying users (₹3–8k/mo fixed costs). That is around month 3–6 in the base case on subscriptions alone, and earlier with ads.

  6. Beyond subscriptions, ranked by revenue ÷ effort:

    1. A "Studio" prosumer tier for interior designers and carpenters (₹399/mo, ₹2,999/yr).
    2. The B2B "does it fit?" widget: the biggest absolute revenue and still the real business, but sales-heavy.
    3. Affiliate "buy what fits" links: easy, but they must stop when B2B deals start.
    4. One-time paid reports: small.

  7. Top risks:

    1. Cloud-credit cost overrun and refund abuse.
    2. India's low and episodic conversion.
    3. The DPDP Act for uploaded room imagery.

    Play policy on paywalls and ads in AR flows is manageable with the placement rules in §5.1.

1. Free vs Pro feature split

Principles:

  • Accuracy is never a Pro feature. If Pro measured "better", every Free review would say the app is inaccurate.
  • Never take a result hostage. A measurement or scan the user has made is always visible, placeable and shareable. Pro gates new capacity and richer output, not data the user already has.
  • Check gates before a task starts, never mid-AR. A paywall never appears inside a live camera session (§5.1).
  • Free must be complete for the core job: measure the space, scan or enter the item, place it, get the verdict, share it on WhatsApp.
Feature Free Pro Why there
Measure (all modes), ±, quality badge, calibration, history Unlimited Same Reputation. Paywalling it would poison reviews and the B2B pitch
Smart Measure (auto rectangles, hands-free drop, object naming) Included + batch mode: measure many items in a row, auto-named, exported as a list/CSV Auto-rectangle reduces user error, so it is an accuracy feature and stays free. Batch output is a prosumer convenience
Object scan (measured proxy) and Place in room + Fits/Tight/Too big Included Same This is the hero and the demand engine for B2B and affiliate. Gating it would kill the funnel
Ads Capped interstitial after Save→Done; banners only on History/Gallery/Settings; never on camera screens (business.md §2.3, ui-ux.md §5) None Ad-free is the #1 reason utility users pay
Gallery size (saved objects) 20 items; later each rewarded ad gives +5, up to 40. Over the cap: view, place, share and delete still work; only new saves are blocked, checked before a scan starts, with a visible "18 / 20" counter Unlimited Scans cost us nothing to store (≤ 0.5 MB each, on-device), so the cap is purely a conversion lever. 20 covers a typical home project; heavy users are the ones who pay
Exports Share image with dimensions overlay at standard resolution, with a small "Measured with SpaceView" watermark (a WhatsApp growth loop). Rewarded ad (v1.x) = one PDF PDF with dimensions (multi-item, notes, ± shown), hi-res unwatermarked image, CSV, GLB/USDZ 3D export Output is what designers, carpenters and shoppers pay for. The watermark is free marketing
Room / floor plan (v1.x) 1 room, view and share as an image Unlimited rooms, PDF floor plan with dimensions, saved projects One room proves value; renovators and designers need many
Furniture templates (sofa, bed, wardrobe from guided measurements) 3 core templates (straight sofa, bed, wardrobe), full accuracy, saved within the gallery cap Full library: L-sofa, sectional, recliner, bunk bed, TV unit, dining set, modular wardrobe with door-swing clearance; colours and materials Core templates make the free fit-check honest. The long tail is a nice-to-have
On-device rough 3D scan (v1.x) 3 saved 3D scans (count toward the gallery) Unlimited + GLB export Zero marginal cost, so meter it for conversion only
Cloud photo-real 3D scan (v1.x) No free scans. Can buy credit packs. Sample cloud scans visible in the app Included monthly credits (§3) + packs Each scan costs real GPU money (₹5–40), and ad revenue (~₹0.13–0.18 per rewarded view [est.]) cannot fund one
Multi-item compare (2+ items against one space, side by side) Compare 2 items, not saved Unlimited, saved comparisons, shareable "Which of these 3 sofas fits?" is a strong purchase-moment upsell
Cloud backup / sync (v1.x) Local backup file export and import (zip), free forever Automatic cloud backup, restore on a new phone A free local file keeps data portable (good for reviews and DPDP). Automatic sync costs us storage and an account system
Units, EN/HI, accessibility, WhatsApp share Included Included Table stakes

Pro value at v1 launch, before cloud features exist: no ads, unlimited gallery, PDF / hi-res / GLB exports, full template library (if templates ship in v1, otherwise at v1.x), unlimited compare, and Smart batch mode. That is enough to sell an ad-free, output-oriented Pro. Cloud scans and backup arrive as added value in v1.x, which also gives a renewal story.

2. Pricing for India

2.1 Recommended price points

Product (Play type) Price Net (GST-registered) Net (pre-GST) Notes
Pro Monthly (auto-renew base plan) ₹129 ₹90.0 ₹109.7 No trial on monthly. Monthly is the price anchor
Pro Yearly (auto-renew base plan) ₹799 (≈ ₹67/mo, "48% off") ₹557 (₹46.4/mo) ₹679 7-day free trial, new customers only
Pro 30-day Pass (prepaid base plan, no auto-renew) ₹149 ₹103.9 ₹126.7 For the episodic "I'm buying furniture now" user. UPI-friendly; can be topped up [9]
Pro Lifetime (one-time, non-consumable), v1.x ₹1,999 ₹1,394 ₹1,699 All on-device Pro features for life; no included cloud credits (buy packs). Launch only once churn data exists, around month 4–6, ideally as a win-back or exit offer
Credit packs (consumable), v1.x 3 for ₹99, 10 for ₹299 (base case) ₹23.0 / ₹20.9 per credit ₹28.1 / ₹25.4 Sold to Free and Pro. See §3 for the cost-dependent table
Studio (separate subscription, prosumer), v1.x ₹399/mo, ₹2,999/yr ₹278 / ₹174 per mo ₹339 / ₹212 Designers and carpenters: branded PDF, client projects, DXF, 10 or 8 credits/mo (§4)

Why ₹129 / ₹799 rather than ₹99 / ₹599:

  • The scope has grown. Pro now covers templates, 3D and cloud features, not just ad removal.
  • At ₹599/yr the net is only ₹35/mo after Play and GST, which leaves no room for even one ₹15 cloud scan a month at a healthy margin.
  • Indian anchors are higher:
    • Spotify Premium Individual was cut to ₹139/mo in May 2026 [20].
    • Google One 100 GB is about ₹130/mo [21].
    • YouTube Premium Lite is ₹89/mo [22].
    • RevenueCat's IN/SEA median subscription price is $3.75/mo and $18.32/yr (≈ ₹330 and ₹1,610) [1]. SpaceView would still sit well below the median.
  • Keep ₹99 / ₹599 as the A/B "low arm". Run it as separate base plans served by Remote Config to new installs. Switch if it lifts paying users by at least 35%, since 129 ÷ 99 ≈ 1.30 plus a margin for cloud costs [est.].

Regional pricing:

  • Set INR prices by hand; do not use Play's auto-conversion from USD. Play's INR price range is ₹4–₹30,000 [10].
  • Use psychological endings (₹129 / ₹149 / ₹799).
  • For other markets, start from Play's suggested local prices, but keep the US at about $2.99/mo and $19.99/yr. That is still well under Polycam, magicplan and AR Ruler (§5.4) and keeps the Indian price from looking like an arbitrage.
  • UPI Autopay has worked for Play subscriptions in India since November 2022 [8], so no extra work is needed.

Trial length:

  • RevenueCat (Aug 2025–Jul 2026) finds utilities convert best with 10–16-day trials (47.4%) [2]. Trials of 17–32 days convert at 42.5% against 25.5% for trials of four days or fewer, across all apps [3].
  • SpaceView's job is episodic: a user measures a wall, buys a TV, done. A long trial lets the whole job finish free.
  • Recommendation: start with 7 days on Yearly only, and A/B test 7 vs 14 days once 1,000 or more trials have started.
  • The trial includes 1 cloud credit, not the full allowance, so trial farming has bounded cost.
  • Play enforces offer eligibility per Google account via the developer's eligibility rule ("new customer acquisition"). Set it to never had this subscription [17].

2.2 Google Play service fees, as they apply to SpaceView (verified 5 Nov 2026)

Google's official fee page [4] now has two schedules.

India (all "remaining markets"), today EEA / UK / US (from 30 Jun 2026) and AU / JP (from 30 Sep 2026)
Auto-renewing subscriptions 15% from day one, regardless of revenue 10% service fee + 5% billing fee = 15%
One-time products (credit packs, lifetime) 15% on the first $1M/yr, only if enrolled in the 15% programme, otherwise 30% 20% + 5% = 25% for new installs; 25% + 5% = 30% for existing installs (lower in the Apps Experience programme)
Alternative / user-choice billing Fee reduced by 4 percentage points (subscriptions 11%) [4][5] Separate external-offers schemes

What this means for SpaceView:

  1. Enrol in the 15% service-fee programme in Play Console before the first sale. Otherwise one-time products are charged at 30%.
  2. Subscriptions cost 15% under either schedule, so put most value in the subscription, including cloud credits. Google says the new schedule will roll out globally but gives no India date [4]. If India moves, credit packs would go from 15% to 25–30%. That is why §3 prices packs with headroom and favours included credits.
  3. User-choice billing (India) is not worth it for v1. It saves 4 percentage points, but you then pay your own payment gateway (≈ 2% [est.]), integrate a second checkout, and receive service-fee invoices from Google [5][19]. Developer bodies also called the residual fee "still high" [5]. Revisit only above about ₹5 lakh/mo of in-app revenue.
  4. Tax:
    • Play prices must include all taxes.
    • Indian developers are responsible for their own GST, and must give Google a GSTIN if registered [6].
    • Google withholds 1% TDS (with PAN) and, where applicable, GST TCS from Indian developers' proceeds [7].
    • Model net = price × 85% − price × 18/118 ≈ 69.75% of list once GST-registered, and 85% before. B2B invoices will force registration early, so plan on the 69.75%. Confirm the GST registration threshold and the TCS treatment with a CA.
  5. Play Billing Library 8.0+ has been mandatory for app updates since 31 Aug 2026 (extensions ended 1 Nov 2026) [18]. Build Pro on PBL 9.

3. Cloud-scan credits: the model that keeps heavy users profitable

3.1 Design

Definitions:

  • 1 credit = 1 standard cloud scan. A standard scan is a single object or furniture piece, within an image and video cap.
  • A Room or HQ scan = 3 credits.
  • The credits-per-scan table is published in the app and fixed at the time of purchase. Play's subscriptions policy forbids misleading users or reducing the recurring value after purchase [11].

Included credits are granted monthly, even on Yearly. Annual plans never receive 12 months of credits up front, which limits refund-and-spend abuse (§5.2).

Rollover:

  • Unused included credits roll over up to 2× the monthly allowance, then stop accruing.
  • They lapse when the subscription ends; this is disclosed on the paywall.
  • Purchased pack credits never expire. They are a paid good and expiring them invites complaints.

Charging rules:

  • A credit is charged only when a job succeeds. Failed jobs refund the credit automatically.
  • One free re-run is allowed per scan if quality checks fail.

Free users: no included credits. They can buy packs, and they see sample cloud scans.

Trial: 1 credit.

3.2 Base-case allowances (₹15 per standard scan)

Plan Included credits Rollover cap Max cloud cost per month
Pro Monthly ₹129 3 / month 6 ₹45
Pro Yearly ₹799 2 / month (24/yr) 4 ₹30
Pro 30-day Pass ₹149 3 — ₹45
Studio ₹399/mo · ₹2,999/yr 10 / month · 8 / month 20 · 16 ₹150 · ₹120
Trial (7 days) 1 — ₹15
Packs (anyone) 3 for ₹99 · 10 for ₹299 Never expire Paid for

3.3 Sensitivity: allowances and pack prices per cost scenario

Pack pricing rule: a pack needs a list price of at least cost ÷ (0.6975 × 0.70) ≈ 2.05 × cost per credit to earn a 30% margin after Play's 15% and GST. That works out to ₹10 per credit at a ₹5 scan, ₹31 at ₹15, and ₹82 at ₹40. Friendly price points land at 28–35% at the base cost. Included allowances are set so that a user who uses every credit still leaves at least about 25–30% gross margin on every plan.

Cost per standard scan Monthly ₹129 Yearly ₹799 Pass ₹149 Studio mo / yr Packs
₹5 5 / mo 4 / mo 5 25 / 20 5 for ₹99 · 15 for ₹249
₹15 (base) 3 / mo 2 / mo 3 10 / 8 3 for ₹99 · 10 for ₹299
₹25 2 / mo 1 / mo 2 6 / 4 2 for ₹99 · 6 for ₹299
₹40 1 / mo 6 / yr (one every 2 months) 1 4 / 3 1 for ₹99 · 4 for ₹349

If the real cost comes in above ₹25:

  • Make cloud scans a Studio-first feature.
  • Keep a single ₹99 "pay per scan" option for consumers.
  • Push the cheaper on-device 3D scan as the Pro default.

KIRI Engine already gives free, unlimited photo scans [15], so a paid cloud scan must offer what KIRI does not: true scale with ±, the fit verdict, and placement in your room. A bare mesh is not enough.

3.4 Rule for the orchestrator once 3d-capture.md lands

With c = the measured all-in cost per standard scan (GPU + storage + egress, at p90, not the median), and net = the plan's net revenue per month (Monthly ₹90, Yearly ₹46.4, Pass ₹104, Studio ₹278 / ₹174):

  • allowance ≤ (0.75 × net − 3) / c, rounded down to a friendly number. This keeps a worst-case gross margin of at least 25%.
  • Cap the allowance at 5/month on Monthly and 4/month on Yearly even when scans are cheap, so users are not anchored on near-free cloud scans.
  • pack_price_per_credit ≥ 2.05 × c, giving about 30% margin.

This rule reproduces the §3.3 table. Re-check every quarter; GPU prices fall.

4. Unit economics

4.1 Benchmarks used

Metric Value Source
Download→paid within 35 days, IN/SEA users median 0.7%, top quartile >1.9% (N. America 2.8%, APAC 2.4%) RevenueCat SOSA 2026 [1]
Download→paid, all apps / low-priced apps median 2.0% / 1.4% [1]
Download→trial, IN/SEA 3.0–3.7% median; Utilities (global) 6.5% [1][2]
Trial→paid, IN/SEA 15.2% (N. America 34.2%) [1]
Hard paywall vs freemium, trial→paid at day 35 10.7% vs 2.1% [3]
Year-1 realised LTV per payer, IN/SEA $14 (≈ ₹1,230), against a $23 global median [1]
India in-app spend ≈ $345 m in Q2 2026, +35% YoY [23]
AdMob India eCPM Banner ~$0.10–0.50; interstitial ~$0.50–2.00; rewarded the highest (one 2026 comparison puts AdMob India at $0.80–1.50 overall). Sources vary by 4× business.md §2.1, [24][25]
Ad revenue per 1k DAU (capped plan) ≈ $54 / month base (₹4.7k) business.md §2.2

Ad yield per Free user: about ₹3.8 per free DAU per month (80% of DAU actually served). At a 12% DAU/MAU ratio, which suits an episodic utility [est.], that is about ₹0.45 per free MAU per month.

4.2 Gross margin per Pro user per month (GST-registered, Play 15%)

Usage levels:

  • Light = no cloud scans.
  • Typical = 1 scan a month. Breakage on included allowances is usually 50–70% [est.].
  • Heavy = the full allowance. Usage above the allowance is paid through packs at ≥ 30% margin, so it adds to profit.

Every case also deducts "Other" (backup storage, verification backend, refunds and support) at ₹3 per payer per month [est.].

Cloud cost / scan Plan (net ₹/mo) Light Typical Heavy (fixed allowance from §3.2) Heavy (allowance re-tuned per §3.3)
₹5 Monthly (₹90) ₹87 · 97% ₹82 · 91% ₹72 · 80% (5 credits) ₹62 · 69%
₹5 Yearly (₹46.4) ₹43 · 94% ₹38 · 83% ₹33 · 72% (4 credits) ₹23 · 50%
₹15 Monthly (₹90) ₹87 · 97% ₹72 · 80% ₹42 · 47% same
₹15 Yearly (₹46.4) ₹43 · 94% ₹28 · 61% ₹13 · 29% same
₹15 Pass (₹103.9) ₹101 · 97% ₹86 · 83% ₹56 · 54% same
₹15 Studio mo (₹278) ₹275 · 99% ₹260 · 94% ₹125 · 45% same
₹40 Monthly (₹90) ₹87 · 97% ₹47 · 52% −₹33 · −37% (1 credit) ₹47 · 52%
₹40 Yearly (₹46.4) ₹43 · 94% ₹3 · 7% −₹37 · −79% (0.5 credit) ₹23 · 50%

Takeaway:

  • With a fixed "3 a month" promise, Pro is loss-making for heavy users at ₹40 per scan. Re-tuning the allowance per §3.3 keeps every plan at ≥ ~30% worst-case and ≥ 50% typical margin.
  • Pre-GST, all nets are about 22% higher.
  • Blended ARPPU for the expected mix of payers in stock at month 12 (≈ 20% monthly, 77% yearly, 3% pass) is ≈ ₹82 gross / ₹57 net per month.
  • This is consistent with RevenueCat's IN/SEA year-1 LTV of about ₹1,230 per payer [1].

4.3 ARPU (all users)

Base case, month 12:

  • Subscriptions ≈ ₹2.1 net per MAU per month (recognised revenue, not cash).
  • Ads ≈ ₹0.45 per free MAU.
  • Affiliate ≈ ₹0.30.
  • Packs ≈ ₹0.10.
  • Consumer ARPU ≈ ₹3.0 net per MAU per month [est.].

For comparison, ads alone yield about ₹0.45, so one Pro payer is worth about 125 free MAU of ad revenue. This is why Pro conversion, not ad density, is the lever.

4.4 Backend break-even

Running costs are about ₹3–8k/month [est.]:

  • Firebase / Cloud Run with scale-to-zero. Cloud Storage costs about $0.026/GB-month after 5 free GB; Firestore has daily free quotas [26].
  • Serverless GPU jobs, billed per second with no idle cost when scaled to zero [27].
  • Domain and website, error monitoring, and CA/GST compliance once registered.

One-off costs:

  • Play developer fee: $25.
  • A DPDP-grade privacy policy and terms from a lawyer: about ₹15–40k [est.].

Break-even at a blended typical contribution of about ₹45 per payer per month:

Fixed cost / month Payers needed …or Free MAU from ads alone Base-case month reached
₹3,000 ≈ 70 ≈ 6,600 Month 3
₹5,000 ≈ 110 ≈ 11,000 Month 4
₹8,000 ≈ 180 ≈ 17,600 Month 6

The last column counts subscriptions alone. Adding ads brings it about one month earlier.

Cloud GPU is variable cost and is covered per credit, so it never enters the fixed-cost line. Founder time is not included.

5. Risks

5.1 Play policy: subscriptions, paywalls and ads in AR flows

Subscriptions policy [11] requires:

  • Clear price, billing period and auto-renew disclosure on the paywall. Do not present the Yearly price mainly as a monthly cost.
  • Trial terms: length, what converts, when, at what price, and how to cancel.
  • A clearly visible dismiss on every paywall.
  • An easy cancellation path, such as a deep link to the Play Subscription Center.
  • No SKU named "Free Trial".
  • Subscriptions must give ongoing value; one-time benefits belong in one-time products. Credit packs are therefore consumables, and included credits are an ongoing monthly benefit.

SpaceView paywall placement rules (extending ui-ux.md §5):

  1. No paywall inside a live AR session. Gates run before the session starts (template picker, "3D scan", gallery-full check) or after the result is saved (export, compare).
  2. Never block a result already computed. A Free user who hits a cap still sees and shares their measurement.
  3. Pro users see no ads anywhere, and no "upgrade" nags appear on camera screens.
  4. Show the soft upsell at most once per session, on a natural break (after Save→Done, alternating with the capped interstitial: never both in one exit).

Ads: as in business.md §2.3 and ui-ux.md §5. Play's disruptive-ads rules treat an ad after a tap that starts an activity as unexpected [12].

Accounts: cloud backup and credits need sign-in, which triggers Play's account-deletion requirement: an in-app deletion path plus a web deletion link, plus the Data safety deletion questions [13]. Keep Free account-less.

5.2 Refunds and abuse of cloud credits

Abuse vector Control
Buy Yearly, spend credits, refund Credits are granted monthly, so a refund exposes at most one month's grant. Server ledger plus Real-time Developer Notifications + Voided Purchases API (refunds, chargebacks, cancellations; 30-day lookback) [14] claw back unspent credits; a negative balance blocks new jobs. Play's user-initiated refund window is 48 hours; after that the developer decides [16]. Publish a refund policy: "pro-rata refunds exclude consumed credits"
Trial farming with many Google accounts Trial gives 1 credit. Play Integrity verdict required for cloud jobs. Per-device and per-account job caps
Pack refund after use Consume the purchase server-side only after the credit is in the ledger. Voided Purchases API → claw back; on repeated abuse, warn then suspend cloud jobs (Google recommends escalating, consistent enforcement [14])
Oversized or huge uploads to force expensive jobs Hard caps on images, video length and resolution per credit tier. Reject before upload
Re-running the same scan repeatedly Dedupe by content hash; one free quality re-run only
Modded app with fake entitlement v1 Pro has no marginal cost, so local verification is acceptable at launch. Cloud jobs always verify the purchase token server-side
Bursty cost spikes Global daily GPU budget with a queue ("ready in ~10 min") rather than unbounded autoscale

5.3 Privacy and the DPDP Act: uploading room and object imagery

Timeline:

  • The DPDP Rules were notified on 13–14 Nov 2025.
  • Consent-manager provisions start around Nov 2026.
  • Core data-fiduciary duties (notice, consent, security safeguards, breach notification, rights, deletion) apply from about 13–14 May 2027 [28][29].
  • Cloud scans will launch inside the window and must be compliant at launch.

Why room imagery matters: photos of rooms can contain faces, children, documents, addresses and screens, so they are personal data.

Penalties: up to ₹250 crore for failing security safeguards, and up to ₹200 crore for children's-data violations [30].

Children: under the DPDP Act, anyone under 18 is a child, and processing their data needs verifiable parental consent (Rule 10) [30].

Controls:

  • On-device by default. Measure, proxy scan, on-device 3D and the local gallery never leave the phone. Only an explicit "Cloud scan" upload does.
  • A separate, specific consent screen before the first upload, covering what is uploaded, its purpose, its retention, the processor's location, and how to delete it.
    • Raw images are deleted within 7 days of a successful job.
    • Only the mesh is kept, and only while it is in the user's cloud gallery.
  • 18+ self-declaration for cloud features, with the Play content rating and target audience set to adults. If minors become a target audience, cloud features need parental-consent flows.
  • Auto-blur faces on-device before upload, or at least warn: "people visible? retake" [est. feasibility].
  • Processor contracts (DPA) with the GPU and storage provider. Keep processing in an Indian region where available. Cross-border transfer is allowed unless the government restricts the destination [29].
  • Security: encryption in transit and at rest, signed upload URLs, least-privilege service accounts, and a breach-notification runbook.
  • Data safety form: declare photos, user content and account data, list uploads as optional, and give the deletion path [13].

5.4 Competitor pricing (verified Nov 2026 where possible)

Competitor Model and current price Source Implication for SpaceView
Polycam Free: 10 photogrammetry + 3 AI captures, 150 images per capture. Basic $30/mo or $150/yr; Business $400/yr per user; Enterprise $1,200/yr per user (3-user minimum). All scenarios share these assumptions:
