Reviewsdocs/reviews/M0-M1-review.md · updated 03 Nov 2026, 11:10

Review: M0 (scaffold and CI) + M1 (:engine), commit f6ac685

Reviewer agent, 2026-11-03. I read every file in engine/src/main, all engine tests, the CI workflow, the Gradle files, ci/check_native_libs.py, the manifest (source and merged release) and the R8 setup. I checked them against docs/PLAN.md, docs/research/measurement-engine.md and docs/research/android-performance.md.

How the findings were verified. Each suspicion was tested with a throwaway JUnit probe, which has since been deleted. The tree is clean apart from other agents' work in progress. The probe numbers below come from that run. GLB validity was checked with the official Khronos gltf-validator 2.0.0-dev.3.10, run from the scratchpad. After the probe was deleted, :engine:test ran green with all 119 tests passing.

Line numbers refer to commit f6ac685. Builder B's uncommitted M5 work has since changed android.yml, app/build.gradle.kts and the manifest, so lines in those files may have shifted. The engine/ and ci/ folders are unchanged.

Summary

Severity Count
BLOCKER 0
MAJOR 5
MINOR 17

The maths is mostly correct and carefully written. The median/MAD, quickselect, Jacobi eigen-solver, Hartley normalisation, quaternion and pose algebra, correlated-scale propagation for area and volume, the fit-verdict bands and the feet-inches carry are all correct. The GLB passes the Khronos validator with 0 errors and 0 warnings.

The real risk is honesty of the ±. The engine's ± reaches about 95% only if every caller adds terms the API does not ask for. The autocorrelation correction under-covers on realistic, correlated AR samples. Neither problem is a bug in the arithmetic. Both mean that, as wired today, the app would show ranges that are too small. Fix them before M2 wires :ar into the engine.

1. engine/src/main correctness

MAJOR-1: The public distance and rectangle APIs give about 32% coverage, not 95%, unless the caller remembers to add endpoint-localisation error

Uncertainty.kt:189 (UncertaintyModel.distance), Uncertainty.kt:164-186, PointEstimator.kt:184, Shapes.kt:66

PointEstimate.point.covariance is the standard error of the mean of per-frame jitter. The user's aiming or tap error and the hit-test bias stay the same for the whole 0.5–1 s hold, so averaging cannot see them. distance(), rectangle() and Calibrator take that covariance as the whole random error. The only thing that adds the localisation term is an optional withExtraSigma(), and the only test that proves 95% coverage (displayedBoundCoversTruth) calls it by hand.

Failing scenario (probe). Endpoints have a 3 mm per-axis aim error (about 1.5 px at 1 m on VGA) plus 1 mm frame jitter, 30 samples, NO_DEPTH tier, fed through PointEstimator → model.distance():

True length Mean shown ±95 Actual coverage
8.56 cm (card) ±1.8 mm 32%
30 cm ±6.0 mm 75%
1 m ±2.0 cm 92% (scale term dominates)

The same flaw feeds calibration. A card or A4 measured this way reports a factor σ that is far too small. The TOO_NOISY gate (Calibration.kt:148) then never trips, and the calibrated ± falls to the 0.3% floor, which the user sees as a "precise" ±0.6%.

Fix. Make the safe path the only path:

  • Have distance, height, rectangle and boxFromPoints take an endpoint type that carries the localisation inputs (range, focal px and pixel error, or an explicit σ), for example EndpointObservation(estimate, rangeM, focalPx, pixelSigma).
  • Alternatively, give UncertaintyModel a required minEndpointSigma(range) that is always added in quadrature.
  • Also consider the research's absolute floor ("never better than ±1 cm" for non-LiDAR Android) as a tier-level absFloor95.
  • Add a test that runs the scenario above and requires coverage ≥ 0.9. It fails today.

MAJOR-2: The autocorrelation correction under-covers on correlated AR samples (55–82% instead of 95%)

PointEstimator.kt:179-184, PointEstimator.kt:205-206 (MAX_RHO = 0.9)

Consecutive AR frames are strongly correlated: hand tremor and slow VIO wobble are low-frequency. Probe: AR(1) jitter with σ = 3 mm, 30 samples, checking the true point against ±1.96·SE along x:

ρ Coverage Mean n_eff
0.5 0.90 12.5
0.8 0.82 5.9
0.9 0.70 4.3
0.95 0.55 3.6

There are four causes:

  1. The lag-1 ρ estimate from 30 samples is biased low (about −(1+4ρ)/n).
  2. ρ is capped at 0.9.
  3. With strong correlation, the sample variance itself underestimates the process variance.
  4. k = 1.96 is used even when n_eff is about 3–6 (Student-t would be about 2.6–4.3).

Existing tests miss this. standardErrorIsCalibrated turns the correction off and uses i.i.d. data. autocorrelationInflatesStandardError only checks that the SE grows.

Fix. Any of these closes most of the gap:

  • Use the bias-corrected ρ: ρ̂ + (1+4ρ̂)/n.
  • Raise the cap to about 0.97.
  • Apply the variance correction to sampleCov as well: multiply by 1/(1 − 2ρ/(n(1−ρ))).
  • Use a Student-t factor for n_eff < 30. Simplest of all, floor nEff at a physically motivated maximum, for example "≤ 1 independent sample per 100 ms of hold".

Add an AR(1) coverage test at ρ = 0.9 that requires ≥ 0.9.

MAJOR-3: The rectangle badge ignores geometric consistency, so a wrong number can be shown as FAIR or GOOD

Shapes.kt:39, Shapes.kt:66-116

RectangleMeasurement.quality is worst(width, height). Neither of those reflects corner order, squareness or planarity. The research (§2.6) says the angle deviation must feed the uncertainty. Probes:

  • Bow-tie (Z) tap order. Corners TL, TR, BL, BR on a 60 × 40 cm frame give H = 72.1 cm ± 1.5 (true 40 cm), badge FAIR, maxRightAngleDeviationDeg = 56°. Both "height" edges are diagonals and agree with each other, so the half-difference σ guard does nothing.
  • Corners 4 cm off the supplied plane (bad depth or wrong plane), DEPTH_SENSOR tier: planarityRms = 0.04, W = 60 cm ± 0.5, badge GOOD.

Fix.

  • Reorder the snapped corners by angle around their centroid in the plane before computing edges, or reject non-convex and self-intersecting quads, as Homography.rectify already does.
  • Fold maxRightAngleDeviationDeg, diagonalMismatch and planarityRms into the width/height σ, for example σ_geom = ½·|diag mismatch| and planarity × tan(view angle).
  • Force ROUGH when !isSquare or when planarityRms is above about 1 cm.

MAJOR-4: Plane fitting accepts near-collinear (strip) point sets and returns a confident, tilted plane

Plane.kt:260, Plane.kt:145

The degeneracy test only rejects λ₁ ≤ 1e-12·λ₂, which means exactly collinear points. Probe: 200 depth or feature points in a 1 m × 2 cm strip on a wall (a skirting line, a narrow textured band on a plain wall, the edge of a frame), with 3 mm noise. RANSAC returns a fit 200/200 times, with normal tilt up to 8.4°, a small rmsResidual and a tiny offsetStandardError.

At 30 cm from the strip, 8.4° puts the plane about 4.4 cm off. Every ray–plane corner beyond the strip inherits that error, yet the ± contains no term for it. Plain painted walls are the research's top risk (§6.4-1), and these inputs are exactly what such walls produce.

Fix.

  • Reject or flag fits where √λ₁ (in-plane minor extent) is less than about 10 × rmsResidual, or λ₁/λ₀ < about 25.
  • Expose a normal-angle σ ≈ rms / (√λ₁ · √n) in PlaneFit, and propagate it as a lateral term proportional to the distance from the centroid.
  • Add a test: a strip fit must be null or flagged.

MINOR-1: PointEstimator.estimate() can return NaN, and the next engine call crashes

PointEstimator.kt:159-160

The comment "m ≥ 1 always" is false. Being within one MAD holds per axis, not jointly. Probe: with 15 samples, give each axis a different majority subset of exact-equal values so the three subsets have no common member. The result is inlierCount = 0 and a NaN position. model.distance() then throws IllegalArgumentException from RawLength.init, which would happen on the AR frame thread. It is rare, because it needs strongly clustered or quantised samples, but the fix is trivial.

Fix. if (m == 0) → fall back to the per-axis medians with SE = 1.2533·MAD/√n, or return null and mark the estimate unstable.

MINOR-2: planeBiasRel divides by the wrong distance

Uncertainty.kt:259-262

A plane offset δ scales ray-intersected lengths by δ/h, where h is the perpendicular camera-to-plane distance, not the viewing range. If a caller passes the range h/cosθ, the term is too small by cosθ: 0.71 at 45°.

Fix. Take cameraPosition: Vec3 and compute abs(fit.plane.signedDistance(cam)). Also consider adding the plane term inside rectangle() automatically when a PlaneFit is supplied.

MINOR-3: The rectangle snaps corners by orthogonal projection, not ray–plane intersection

Shapes.kt:73-78

A corner off the plane by δ along the viewing ray keeps a lateral error of δ·sinθ after orthogonal projection. Ray intersection, as the research (§2.3 and §6.2) prescribes, removes it. The covariance shown is also wrong, because projection drops the normal component without adding the lateral leak.

Fix. Document that M2 must pass ray-intersected corners (Plane.intersect), or add a rectangleFromRays(rays, planeFit) overload.

MINOR-4: A calibration can be accepted even when it makes the ± wider

Calibration.kt:84, Uncertainty.kt:146-150

MAX_REL_SIGMA = 1% is looser than the NO_DEPTH prior σ (2%/1.96 = 1.02%). Probe: a calibration with relSigma 0.99% is accepted. With the floor the effective σ becomes 1.034%, which is wider than the uncalibrated 1.020%, while the app tells the user they are in "precision mode".

Fix. Accept only when √(relSigma² + floor²) < about 0.7 × the tier prior.

MINOR-5: The calibration factor band and scope differ from the research

Calibration.kt:80-81

  • The research says to apply s only when 0.9 < s < 1.1. The code allows 0.85–1.15. A 12–15% scale error means tracking is broken, so refuse it.
  • UncertaintyModel.withCalibration applies the factor to every measured length in the session. The research says the factor corrects scale "for that plane and session". Depth bias is distance- and plane-dependent (δZ/Z).

Fix. Store the plane and range in ScaleCalibration. Widen the floor or skip the factor for other planes, or for ranges outside about ±50% of the calibration range.

MINOR-6: A box with an unmeasured preset depth and σ = 0 is badged GOOD

Shapes.kt:50, Uncertainty.kt:44

A wall-hung frame with the 2 cm "thin frame" preset passed as RawLength(0.02, 0.0, isMeasured = false) gives a GOOD box. The research (§3.3) says D must be labelled "low confidence" or "estimate".

Fix. Exclude unmeasured dimensions from BoxMeasurement.quality, and expose an explicit isEstimate flag per dimension for the UI.

MINOR-7: FitPolicy.allowRotation also lets the item be tipped onto its side

FitVerdict.kt:73-74

All 3! permutations are tried, so a 180 cm fridge can "fit" a 70 cm-high niche lying down. The test rotationFindsBestOrientation encodes exactly this for 2D (W↔H).

Fix. Add rotation = NONE | ABOUT_VERTICAL | ANY, with ABOUT_VERTICAL as the default. It permutes only the horizontal axes, W and D.

MINOR-8: Homography edge cases

Homography.kt:85-90, Homography.kt:73, Homography.kt:44

  • rectify accepts either winding. Corners given counter-clockwise on screen (TL, BL, BR, TR) produce a valid H that mirrors the texture (verified: accepted). Fix: require the clockwise image-space sign, or reorder the corners.
  • The DLT fixes h₃₃ = 1 in normalised coordinates. This fails if the source centroid maps to infinity. That cannot happen for convex→convex quads, but fromCorrespondences is public. Fix: document this, or add an SVD fallback.
  • det > 1e-300 is not scale-aware. Use a relative test.

Conditioning is fine: 4K-pixel corners give a max reprojection error of 9e-13 px.

MINOR-9: Display rounding chooses the step from the unrounded ±

Units.kt:135-156

A ± of 0.999 cm gives "45.5 cm ± 1.0", so the value shows 0.1 cm resolution against a ±1 cm range. stepFor sees 0.999, whose leading place is 0.1, and the ± is then rounded up to 1.0. Similarly, "0.02 m ± 0.020" shows the ± with more decimals than the value.

Fix. Round the ± to its displayed significant figures first, then derive the step from the rounded value.

Checked and correct: 6′ 0″ (no 5′ 12″) at 71.97 in and 71.99 in, 24 in at 23.97 in, negative values, -0 suppression, Hindi and German locales, and fraction reduction.

MINOR-10: The GLB is valid, with small polish items

GlbWriter.kt:149-152, GlbWriter.kt:164

The Khronos validator reports 0 errors and 0 warnings for textured, untextured and extreme-size boxes (0.1 mm × 50 m). It reports two infos:

  • UNUSED_OBJECT TEXCOORD_0 on the untextured primitive(s).
  • IMAGE_NPOT_DIMENSIONS when the JPEG is not a power of two. Homography.outputSize produces 1024×683 while the sampler asks for LINEAR_MIPMAP_LINEAR.

Manually verified:

  • bufferView offsets are 4-aligned; there is no byteStride (tightly packed); the index accessor offset 12 is a multiple of 2; the image bufferView has no target.
  • POSITION has min/max and they match the floats exactly.
  • JSON padding is 0x20 and BIN padding is 0x00; all fields are little-endian.
  • All 12 triangles are counter-clockwise from outside (r × u = n for every face), so nothing is culled. UV (0,0) is top-left.

Suggestions:

  • Use a separate UV-less attribute set for the sides, or accept the info.
  • Either produce power-of-two textures or use minFilter LINEAR (9729), because Filament mip-maps NPOT textures but some viewers (Scene Viewer) may not.
  • Document that callers must bake JPEG EXIF orientation, since glTF ignores EXIF.
  • Add gltf-validator (npm) to CI on a generated fixture.

MINOR-11: Quality thresholds disagree with the research colour bands

Uncertainty.kt:56-82

The research (§2.10) says green means ≤ 1%. GOOD_ABS = 5 mm gives GOOD to a card at ±4 mm (4.7%). Also, an uncalibrated NO_DEPTH or DEPTH device can never show GOOD for anything over 25–50 cm, because the scale prior alone is 1.5–2%. Both may be intended, but the UI/UX and Judge agents should confirm them before M2, because the badge is the product's main honesty signal.

MINOR-12: Hot-path notes

These are not bugs.

  • PointEstimator is allocation-free per sample, and estimate() allocates only its four result objects. That is fine at 30 fps.
  • PlaneFitter.ransac performs up to 500 iterations × N points, and allocates BooleanArrays and list iterators. With 1–2k depth points that is several milliseconds, so it must run off the GL thread (Dispatchers.Default), not per frame. Say so in its KDoc.
  • Ray.fromPixel assumes the intrinsics and pixel coordinates are in the same (sensor/landscape) orientation. ARCore's getImageIntrinsics() is in sensor orientation, while touch coordinates are in display orientation. Document that the caller must convert with Frame.transformCoordinates2d.

2. Tests

The 119 tests pass. They are well targeted at arithmetic, but several "honesty" tests are tautological: they generate data from exactly the model's own assumptions.

  • UncertaintyTest.displayedBoundCoversTruth and ShapesTest.noisyRectangleIsAccurateAndHonest inject i.i.d. Gaussian noise, draw the scale error from the same tier prior the model uses, and hand the model the exact tap σ they injected. They prove the formulas are internally consistent, not that the ± is honest. Neither covers aim bias that is constant over the hold (MAJOR-1) or correlated frames (MAJOR-2).
  • PointEstimatorTest.standardErrorIsCalibrated disables the autocorrelation correction. autocorrelationInflatesStandardError asserts only that the SE grows by 1.5×, not that coverage is correct.
  • CalibrationTest.calibrationFixesInjectedThreePercentScaleError is good. It is a real end-to-end check, although its corner σ is again the injected one.
  • GlbWriterTest is a thorough structural parse. It would gain from a Khronos-validator run (MINOR-10).

MAJOR-5: The M1 gate "≥ 90% engine test coverage" cannot be verified

engine/build.gradle.kts

No coverage tool (Kover or JaCoCo) is configured, so the Judge cannot check the PLAN §6 M1 gate. Fix: add the Kover plugin to :engine, and run koverVerify with minBound(90) in CI.

Important missing test cases

  1. Coverage of distance and rectangle without withExtraSigma, which fails today (MAJOR-1).
  2. AR(1)-correlated samples at ρ = 0.8, 0.9 and 0.95 (MAJOR-2).
  3. Bow-tie and reversed corner order, and corners far off the supplied plane (MAJOR-3).
  4. RANSAC and least squares on a thin strip, and on two parallel planes (wall plus frame glass 2 cm in front) (MAJOR-4).
  5. A PointEstimator input with zero inliers (MINOR-1).
  6. A calibration that widens the ±, and a factor of 1.12 that should be rejected (MINOR-4 and MINOR-5).
  7. 3D rotation that must not tip the item (MINOR-7).
  8. Mirrored-winding rectify (MINOR-8).
  9. Units at the ± rounding boundaries (0.999 cm, 0.0999 m) and negative feet-inches carry (−11.97 in).
  10. SymmetricEigen3 with repeated eigenvalues, for example a planar disc where λ₁ = λ₂.
  11. A GLB checked by the Khronos validator in CI.

3. Build, CI, manifest and R8

Nothing was found that would definitely break on ubuntu-latest. The workflow mirrors Gridlark's action versions and wrapper (same Gradle 9.6.1 SHA). python3 (3.12) handles int | None with from __future__ import annotations. The zipalign -P 16, aapt2 dump, bundletool get-size total and cut -d, -f2 parsing are all correct. AGP auto-installs SDK 37 on hosted runners.

I could not run the workflow because there is no remote yet. Lint for :ar, :data and :app was not re-run locally because of the RAM limit, so the first CI run is still the real test.

The merged release manifest was verified (app/build/intermediates/merged_manifests/release):

  • The only permissions are CAMERA and the app-private DYNAMIC_RECEIVER_NOT_EXPORTED_PERMISSION, which CI filters out. INTERNET and VIBRATE are correctly removed.
  • android.hardware.camera.ar is required.
  • glEsVersion 0x30000 is required (from SceneView).
  • com.google.ar.core = required overrides SceneView's optional.

R8 runs in full mode with resource shrinking, proguard-android-optimize.txt and no blanket keeps. filamat is excluded and also forbidden in the 16 KB check. The engine uses no reflection, so it needs no keep rules.

MINOR-13: The size gate applies the download budget to the installed size

android.yml:125, android.yml:142

INSTALLED sums the extracted arm64 splits. Native libraries are stored uncompressed (useLegacyPackaging = false), so the installed size is about 1.4–1.6× the download size. In the local release build, the arm64 .so files alone are 6.6 MiB uncompressed and the installed split is roughly 11 MiB, against a 7.2 MiB download. The 18 MiB "download" budget will therefore fail on the installed figure first, around M4/M5 when AdMob arrives, while the real Play download is still about 12 MB.

Fix. Gate on DOWNLOAD only. Report INSTALLED for information, or give it its own larger budget.

MINOR-14: Release-config guard misses the keystore passwords

app/build.gradle.kts:12-19

On tag builds, REQUIRE_RELEASE_CONFIG checks KEYSTORE_FILE but not KEYSTORE_PASSWORD, KEY_ALIAS or KEY_PASSWORD. A tag build with the keystore secret but a missing password fails late in packageRelease with an unclear signing error. Fix: add the three passwords to missing.

MINOR-15: Some CI gates from the research are missing

android.yml:47-53

android-performance.md §7.5 asks for the following, none of which is present:

  • :app:lintRelease. Only lintVitalRelease runs, through checkReleaseBuilds.
  • A dependency guard (a releaseRuntimeClasspath baseline diff).
  • An r8.json artifact extracted from the AAB.
  • A 16 KB-page emulator smoke run.

None of these is needed for M0's gate, but the dependency guard in particular catches mediation SDKs sneaking in at M5.

MINOR-16: The permission pipeline's || true also hides aapt2 failures

android.yml:103-105

|| true masks a failure of aapt2 itself, which would produce an empty list. The following diff still fails the step, so the impact is only a confusing message. Fix: run aapt2 to a file first under set -e, then filter.

MINOR-17: The .gitattributes and gradlew setup is fine but redundant

gradlew is committed as 100755 with LF line endings, and CI also runs chmod +x. No action is needed. This note only closes the HANDOFF's update-index --chmod=+x to-do.

Recommended order of fixes before M2 wiring

  1. MAJOR-1: an endpoint type that carries the localisation σ, plus an absolute floor. Without it every M2 number is over-confident.
  2. MAJOR-2: autocorrelation and Student-t fix, plus a correlated-coverage test.
  3. MAJOR-3 and MAJOR-4: geometric-consistency gating for rectangles and plane fits.
  4. MAJOR-5: Kover with a 90% gate.
  5. The MINOR items can land with M2 or M6.
Reviewsdocs/reviews/M2-review.md · updated 03 Nov 2026, 16:41

M2 review: measure mode, engine fixes, M5 foundations (fbe1479 against f6ac685)

Reviewer: Reviewer agent, 2026-11-03. Scope: git diff f6ac685 fbe1479 (88 files). Source was not edited.

Verdict: 1 BLOCKER, 6 MAJOR, 17 MINOR. The ARCore plumbing is careful: every image, point cloud and raw-depth image is closed on every path, all ARCore reads happen on the frame thread, and the SceneView 4.65 calls match the jar. The problems are in what the ± means once it reaches the user. Its pixel units come from the screen rather than the camera, calibration leaks outside its scope, and the formatter rounds the ± down. There is also one teardown race and one ad-placement rule broken.

How the findings were checked

  • Probe test. A temporary JVM test (ar/src/test/.../TmpReviewProbeTest.kt) built endpoints the way MeasureSession's worker does: 0.3 mm jitter SE, LocalisationInput(range, focal, 2 px). It ran them through MeasurementComposer and Units. The test has been deleted; its output is quoted below. Build lock taken and released.
  • SceneView 4.65.0 bytecode. I disassembled the AAR from the Gradle cache with javap (there is no sources jar). Three things were checked:
    • ARSceneView applies its typed config, then calls sessionConfiguration, then Session.configure, so SpaceView's settings win. Verified.
    • The depthMode re-apply is guarded by a ChangeGate, so it never overrides SpaceView's AUTOMATIC. Verified.
    • Session teardown is ARSession.beginClose: on the main thread it calls onPaused, then posts Session.pause() and Session.close() to an executor (SessionTeardown).
  • ARCore 1.57.0 bytecode. Anchor.detach() calls nativeDetach(session.nativeWrapperHandle, handle). Session.close() does not zero that handle.

Probe output (NO_DEPTH tier)

Case Displayed Engine ±95
Card 85.6 mm at 0.3 m, focal 500 px (VGA camera) 8.6 cm ± 0.4 3.8 mm
same, focal 2000 px (FHD+ view) 8.6 cm ± 0.2 2.1 mm
same, focal 2700 px (QHD+ view) 86 mm ± 2 (badge GOOD) 2.0 mm
Card calibration at 0.3 m, focal 500 REJECTED (TOO_NOISY) n/a
Card calibration at 0.3 m, focal 2000 ACCEPTED, relSigma 0.61% n/a
Height floor → table, calibrated on the table calibrated = true, ± 5.1 mm (uncalibrated ± 15.1 mm) n/a
Floor → wall distance, calibrated on the table calibrated = true n/a
bound95 = 1.45 in (3.7 cm), ROOM "5′ 3″ ± 1″" (−31%) n/a
bound95 = 0.74 in, ROOM "± 1/2″" (−32%) n/a
bound95 = 2.49 cm, OBJECT "60 cm ± 2" (−20%) n/a

BLOCKER

B1. The aim term uses screen pixels, so the ± depends on screen resolution and is several times too small. The tier floor is never applied.

Where

  • ar/.../MeasureSession.kt:270-272: fyView = projM[5] * viewH / 2.
  • MeasureSession.kt:605: LocalisationInput(range, s.focalPx, AIM_PIXELS).
  • MeasureSession.kt:540, 709: ADJUST_PIXELS = 1.0 view pixels.
  • engine/.../Uncertainty.kt:211-215, 233-238: the tier floor applies only to unlocalised endpoints.

What happens

  • The focal length passed to the engine is in view pixels: about 2000 on an FHD+ phone and about 2700 on a QHD+ phone. The engine's model, the research and the M0–M1 probe use camera-image pixels (Uncertainty.pixelLocalisationSigma: "≈2 mm at 1 m on a VGA stream, f≈500 px").
  • So "2 px" here is about 0.5 VGA pixel. The M0–M1 review measured the realistic aim error at about 1.5 VGA pixels (3 mm at 1 m). The modelled aim σ is therefore roughly 3× too small.
  • The aim σ also shrinks as the display gets sharper, although the camera is the same. A QHD+ phone shows a smaller ± than an FHD+ phone with the same sensor.
  • Every AR endpoint carries a LocalisationInput, so DeviceTier.absFloor95 (1 cm, the research rule "never better than ±1 cm on non-LiDAR Android") is never applied on the shipping path.

Failure scenario

  • A bank card at 30 cm shows "86 mm ± 2" with a GOOD badge (probe).
  • With the aim error the M0–M1 review measured, the real 95% band is about ±3–4 mm. Coverage drops back towards the 32–75% that review flagged as MAJOR-1.
  • The same mistake lets a card calibration at 30 cm pass: accepted with 0.61% when the focal is in view pixels, rejected as TOO_NOISY with a camera focal. The UI tells the user to hold the phone about 30 cm away (calib_card_detail), so most card calibrations will report a precision they do not have.
  • Freeze-frame makes it worse. The still comes from the CPU image (long side ≤ 1280, often 640×480), stretched about 3–4× onto the view. ADJUST_PIXELS = 1 view pixel is about ¼ of an image pixel, which no loupe can resolve.

Fix

  1. Express pixel error in camera pixels. Use the GPU-texture focal length (camera.textureIntrinsics.focalLength, the image the user actually sees) for the aim term. Use the CPU-image focal (camera.imageIntrinsics) for ADJUST_PIXELS, because the frozen still is the CPU image.
  2. Until the bench test proves smaller values, enforce tier.absFloor95 as a minimum on every point-to-point length, localised or not: σ_len ≥ absFloor95 / K95.
  3. Re-run the coverage scenarios with these units.

MAJOR

M1. The ± shown is rounded down, up to about 20% (decimal) and about 33% (fractional inches)

Where: engine/.../Units.kt:141, 147-151, 163-169, 268-272. decimalSteps and formatPlusMinus round the ±95 to the nearest displayed step; the inch path deliberately picks "the largest step not exceeding it".

Failure scenario

  • A 1.6 m room span on NO_DEPTH has a ±95 of 1.45 in. AUTO units format it as "5′ 3″ ± 1″" (probe).
  • A 60 cm object with ±2.49 cm shows "± 2".
  • The engine's coverage scenarios are tuned to just over 95% (minimum 95.05%), so every round-down pushes the band the user reads below 95%.

Fix

  • Round the ± up to the display step (ceil, with an epsilon for binary noise), then derive the value step from it. For inches, pick the smallest fraction step ≥ the ±.
  • Add a property test: displayed ± ≥ bound95 across contexts and preferences.

M2. A calibration still applies when the points are on different surfaces

Where

  • ar/.../MeasurementComposer.kt:204, 261-275: sharedPlane returns null when the points' planes differ.
  • engine/.../Calibration.kt:62-74 and Uncertainty.kt:201-204: "unknown plane (null) does not exclude".

Failure scenario: Calibrate with A4 on a table at 0.5 m, then measure floor → table-top height from about 0.6 m.

  • sharedPlane is null because the offset is 75 cm, so appliesTo(null, 0.6) is true.
  • The table's factor is applied: the value changes by 2.25 cm.
  • The ± drops from 15.1 mm to 5.1 mm (probe).
  • Floor → wall distances are treated as calibrated too.

The AR layer turns "points on different surfaces" into "plane unknown", which is exactly the case that should exclude. Research §2.9 says the factor corrects scale "for that plane and session".

Fix: In MeasurementComposer, when model.calibration?.plane != null and sharedPlane(...) == null, use model.withCalibration(null). Keep "unknown does not exclude" only for engine callers that truly lack geometry. Add tests for floor→table and floor→wall.

M3. A calibration from an earlier session is applied forever, to any plane, and claims to be tighter than no calibration

Where

  • app/.../MeasureViewModel.kt:63, 77-78, 117, 126-131
  • data/.../MeasureSettingsRepository.kt:52-56
  • MeasureScreen.kt:75, 204
  • AppNavHost.kt:110-118

What happens

  • Cross-session use. Calibrating from Home or onboarding goes through the CALIBRATE route, which has its own ViewModel. The calibration is therefore always cross-session when used in Measure.
  • Scope and floor across sessions. The plane scope is dropped and only the ±50% range scope remains. The floor is 0.8%, which is still below the NO_DEPTH prior of 1.02% σ.
  • No expiry and no way to clear it. A record never expires, and Settings has no "clear calibration" yet (TODO in HANDOFF).
  • Independent session scale errors. If VIO scale errors are independent between sessions (σ ≈ 1%), applying an old factor gives error s₂ − s₁. That has σ ≈ 1.4%, worse than not calibrating, while the ± shrinks.
  • Session id is the ViewModel's visit time, not the ARCore session. "Try again" after a session failure (sessionKey++), or a configuration change outside configChanges (locale, fontScale), creates a new ARCore world frame with the same id. The old plane and anchor, which are world coordinates, are then compared in the wrong frame, with the tight 0.3% floor. On a horizontal plane only the height matters. A new session starts at about the same phone height, so the stale plane can easily "match".

Fix

  • Until the bench test shows that scale carries over between sessions, apply a stored calibration only inside the ARCore session that produced it. Make the session id an ARCore-session identity: a counter bumped in onSessionCreated, held by MeasureSession, not by the ViewModel.
  • Otherwise show "Calibrated earlier, recalibrate" and use the uncalibrated model.
  • Add expiry, and add the Settings "clear calibration" toggle.
  • Only show the "Calibrated" chip (MeasureHud.kt:95-96) when the current reading is calibrated.

M4. The fitted surface overrides a nearer depth or feature hit and can land behind the object

Where

  • ar/.../MeasureSession.kt:591-603
  • LocalSurfaceModel.kt:101-113 (the cone accepts points at any depth along the ray)
  • FrameReaders.kt:78-82

What happens: FrameReaders.centreHit deliberately prefers a depth or feature hit that is clearly nearer than the ARCore plane (the box-on-floor case). The worker then discards that choice: for any non-PLANE hit it fits RANSAC to every cloud and depth point within the cone, whatever their depth, and takes the fitted surface first.

Failure scenario: Aim at the top of a 40 cm box on a textured floor from 1.2 m.

  • The cone (radius 8 cm) holds box-top and floor points.
  • If the floor supplies 60% or more of the inliers, the fit is the floor, and the endpoint is placed about 40 cm behind the box top.
  • planeBiasRel does not cover this, because it measures the fit's own noise, not choosing the wrong surface.
  • A frame hung 2–3 cm off a wall shows a smaller version of the same effect: a few % length bias.

Fix

  • Reject a fitted intersection whose range differs from the raw hit distance by more than about max(2 cm, 3%). Better, only feed points within a depth band around the raw hit into RANSAC.
  • Keep the fit only to refine the raw hit, never to replace it with a different depth layer.

M5. Anchors are detached while SceneView is pausing or closing the session on another thread

Where: ar/.../MeasureSession.kt:442-454, app/.../MeasureScreen.kt:76-77 against 147.

What happens

  • DisposableEffect(session) { close() } is registered before ARSceneView. Compose forgets effects in reverse order, so SceneView's dispose runs first.
  • ARCore.destroy() leads to ARSession.beginClose(). That calls onPaused on main, then hands Session.pause() and Session.close() to the SessionTeardown executor.
  • Next, on main, MeasureSession.close() calls anchor.detach() for every endpoint. Each call is nativeDetach(session.nativeWrapperHandle, …), a handle that close() does not zero. These calls therefore run concurrently with, or after, Session.close().
  • runCatching cannot catch a native fault.

Failure scenario: Place two points and press back. Depending on executor timing, the result is a native crash or undefined behaviour in ArAnchor_detach while the session is being destroyed. This is intermittent and only visible on a device.

Fix: Do not detach in close(), because Session.close() releases every anchor. Just drop the references. Alternatively, register the MeasureSession DisposableEffect after ARSceneView so it is disposed first, while the session is still alive. Add this to the device checklist: exit with points placed, 20 times.

M6. An interstitial can show on every exit from Measure, even with nothing saved, and it starts while the AR screen is still on screen

Where: app/.../nav/AppNavHost.kt:97-102, ads/AdsManager.kt:217-254, measure/MeasureScreen.kt:109-118.

What happens

  • close calls maybeShowAfterSave(BACK) whenever the Measure route is popped. That includes back from the permission or ARCore gate, and a visit with zero saves.
  • AdPolicy only counts lifetime scansSinceInterstitial. After the grace period, a user who saved 3 measurements yesterday opens Measure, cannot get tracking, presses back, and gets an interstitial.
  • That breaks the rule in docs/research/business.md and ui-ux §5.2: only after Save → Done, at a natural break. It also breaks the NextAction contract in AdPolicy.
  • ad.show() runs one DataStore read after popBackStack(). Navigation-compose's default 700 ms exit transition keeps the ARSceneView composed and drawing camera frames, so the ad opens over a still-visible AR screen.

Fix

  • Keep savedThisVisit in MeasureViewModel, set on Saved. Only request the ad when it is true.
  • Trigger the ad from the destination screen (Home or History) once the transition has settled and the AR entry is gone, for example a one-shot pendingInterstitial flag consumed by LaunchedEffect on Home.
  • Add an explicit "Done" on the Saved confirmation so NextAction.DONE is real.

MINOR

  1. The saved value is not the one the user saw. MeasureScreen.kt:202 saves session.finalReading(), recomputed from the current anchor poses. The ResultCard shows the worker's state.reading (MeasureHud.kt:126-127). History can then read "60.4 ± 0.5" after the card showed "60.2 ± 0.5". Fix: save the exact Reading that was displayed, or display finalReading().

  2. Save is possible while points are not tracking (MeasureHud.kt:126 against 167). Fix: disable Save, or warn, when !state.pointsTracking.

  3. The worker overwrites main-thread state. MeasureSession.kt:657: _state.value = next, built from a buffered FrameSample (channel of 4), overwrites main-thread updates (pointCount, pendingCommit, reading = null after clear()). The ResultCard can flicker back for 1–3 frames after Save, Undo or a commit, and isComplete toggles, which drops ResultCard's rememberSaveable label. Fix: stamp samples with an endpoints generation counter and drop stale ones, or merge only the worker-owned fields.

  4. Pre-loss samples can be committed after a short tracking loss. SampleStabilizer.kt:56-58: a NOT_TRACKING frame arrives with position == null (the worker skips the hit), so the estimator is not reset until 5 misses. After a loss of under 5 frames, the warm-up frames (LIMITED) recompute cached from pre-loss samples, still marked stable and committable during the warm-up the gate meant to enforce. Fix: pass the quality through and reset on NOT_TRACKING even without a position. Do not expose a commit candidate while LIMITED.

  5. The plane anchor may go on the wrong plane. MeasureSession.kt:496-506: lastPlaneHit can be a different plane from the candidate's. isPoseInPolygon ignores height, so a table-top point can get anchored to the floor plane and later move with floor refinements. Fix: check that |plane.centerPose.distanceToPlane(pose)| < 1 cm, or keep the trackable with the candidate.

  6. The surface buffer is smaller than the cloud copy. LocalSurfaceModel keeps 768 points (LocalSurfaceModel.kt:48), but up to 2048 cloud points (MeasureSession.kt:718) plus depth points are pushed per update. Most of each cloud is overwritten at once, and points near the reticle are dropped arbitrarily. Fix: cone-filter before buffering, or size the buffer to match.

  7. A failed freeze capture can throw out of onFrame. FrameReaders.kt:182-192: toNv21 and transformCoordinates2d inside image.use are not guarded, so an exception there escapes onFrame into SceneView's frame callback. Fix: wrap the body in try/catch and return null.

  8. Allocation per frame. About 2×FloatArray(16), 2×DoubleArray(16) per ViewProjection, endpoints.map, FrameSample, the cloud copy, and in DepthPatch.toWorldPoints about 2 Vec3 per pixel (up to about 900 per depth frame). This is acceptable at 30 fps but causes GC churn on low-end devices. Fix: reuse matrices and back-project into primitive arrays.

  9. I/O failures crash the app. These run in scopes with no handler:

    • MeasureViewModel.save and applyCalibration (MeasureViewModel.kt:82-104): Room or DataStore exceptions.
    • AdsManager.recordCompletedScan (AdsManager.kt:204-209): DataStoreAdCounterStore.update does not catch IOException.

    With disk full they crash the app. Fix: catch and report.

  10. The banner reloads on every scroll. HistoryScreen.kt:119-121 with BannerAd.kt:61-81: the AdView is remembered inside a LazyColumn item, so scrolling it off and back destroys it and fires a new ad request each time. Fix: hoist the AdView to the screen, or place the banner outside the lazy list, pinned under it.

  11. An interstitial is preloaded during AR. AdsManager.kt:204-208: recordCompletedScan (called from the AR screen) preloads an interstitial (WebView, network) while ARCore is tracking, which costs memory and CPU on mid-range phones. Fix: defer the preload until the AR screen has been left.

  12. Database not ready for migrations. data/: Room v1 has its schema exported, which is good, but there is no room-testing / MigrationTestHelper set up before M3 adds the Gallery tables. History rows also lack the canonical-record fields from research §6: device tier, ARCore version, calibration factor and scope, raw lengths. Fix: add room-testing and an androidTest for 1→2, and add those columns in the M3 migration.

  13. Manifest permissions. The merged manifest has FOREGROUND_SERVICE and WAKE_LOCK (allow-listed in ci/allowed-permissions.txt), most likely from the ads SDK's WorkManager. Play's foreground-service declaration (target 34+) may then be required. Fix: confirm the source with :app:processReleaseManifest and remove them with tools:node="remove" if no foreground service is used. Also, AndroidManifest.xml:5 still says "Camera is the only permission".

  14. Missing configChanges entries. AndroidManifest.xml:62 lacks locale|layoutDirection|fontScale. Any of these recreates the activity and with it the ARCore session; see M3 for the calibration side effect.

  15. SceneView's blanket keep rules cost size. Its proguard.txt ships -keep class com.google.android.filament.** { *; }, com.google.ar.core.** and dev.romainguy.kotlin.math.**. There is no crash risk, since everything JNI-reachable is kept, but it defeats the "no blanket keeps" intent in app/proguard-rules.pro and costs dex size. Fix: strip it at M6 with -ignorecustomproguardfiles-style filtering or a narrowed rule set, then verify on a device.

  16. CI memory settings. CI reuses org.gradle.jvmargs=-Xmx1536m, sized for the 2 GB local PC, while lintDebug for three modules plus lintRelease run in one daemon. Metaspace and heap OOM was already seen locally. Fix: override on CI, for example GRADLE_OPTS / -Dorg.gradle.jvmargs=-Xmx4g -XX:MaxMetaspaceSize=1g. The workflow is otherwise correct for ubuntu: bash with pipefail, the bundletool checksum, the size-gate parsing, the keystore guard and the tag-only REQUIRE_RELEASE_CONFIG.

  17. "Calibrated" state shown out of scope. The "Calibrated" chip and the calibratedThisVisit tip suppression (MeasureHud.kt:95, ResultCards.kt:137) are keyed on any stored record, not on whether this reading is calibrated. Fix: key them on reading.calibrated.

Checked and OK

  • Native resource release. acquirePointCloud, acquireRawDepthImage16Bits, acquireRawDepthConfidenceImage and acquireCameraImage are all closed through use on every path, including early returns and exceptions.
  • Frame lifetime. No Frame is kept beyond onFrame.
  • ARCore threading. All ARCore reads happen in onSessionUpdated on the main thread. The worker only sees copied primitives and engine types.
  • Coordinate conversions are right. ViewProjection uses the column-major VP inverse. The depth patch uses the TEXTURE_NORMALIZED mapping with texture intrinsics scaled to the depth image and the sensor pose, as ARCore's raw-depth sample does. The still uses a VIEW→IMAGE_PIXELS affine. Hit tests are in view pixels, matching the display geometry.
  • Session configuration. SessionSetup.configure runs after SceneView's typed defaults (verified in bytecode). Instant Placement is off and InstantPlacementPoint hits are ignored.
  • Calibration flow model. Calibration measures with the uncalibrated model. The factor band, skew and TOO_NOISY gates are wired, and the calibration file is excluded from cloud backup and device transfer.
  • Ads setup.
    • Consent: canRequestAds() is checked before MobileAds.initialize, which runs off the main thread after the first frame.
    • Ad IDs: debug always uses Google test IDs, and tag builds fail without real IDs.
    • Counting: recordCompletedScan is called once per successful save, with no double counting (it can under-count if the screen is left before the insert finishes, which is harmless).
    • Banners: never on camera screens.
  • Startup. Application.onCreate is empty and every dependency is lazy.
  • AR Required manifest. camera.ar is required and the com.google.ar.core meta-data is replaced.
  • Hindi strings. HANDOFF says lint passes on values-hi. I did not check the strings one by one.
Reviewsdocs/reviews/M2-gate.md · updated 03 Nov 2026, 22:52

M2 gate: measure mode (Final Reviewer + Judge)

Judge, 2026-11-03. Gate commit: 10f7abd (fixes for the M2 review of fbe1479). I checked it against docs/PLAN.md §4, §6 and §7, HANDOFF.md, docs/reviews/M0-M1-review.md, docs/reviews/M2-review.md and docs/TESTING.md. No source was edited.

Verdict: PASS WITH CONDITIONS

All 1 BLOCKER and 11 MAJOR findings from the two reviews are fixed in the code, not just claimed. The ± now always includes the tier floor and is expressed in camera pixels. Calibration is scoped to its plane and to its own ARCore session. Anchors are never detached after a pause. Interstitials only follow a save, and never over the camera.

Three small honesty gaps remain:

  • R1. A rounding step in the composer can show a ± a few micrometres below the engine's bound.
  • R2. The live AR value is visible while the user measures the known-length span, so the calibration can be made circular.
  • R3. Home and Settings say "Calibrated on …" for a calibration that no longer applies.

Each is a one-to-ten-line fix. Apply R1–R4 and rebuild the debug APK before the user does the bench test (PLAN §7). No new review round is needed: the orchestrator can verify the fixes with the tests named below.

How this was verified

  • I read every changed file in git diff fbe1479 HEAD, plus the unchanged code paths each fix depends on.
  • Probe test (ar/src/test/.../TmpGateProbeTest.kt, deleted after the run):
    • Setup: 20,000 random readings (all modes, all tiers, half with an in-scope calibration, worker-style endpoints using AimModel).
    • Each reading was formatted in every MeasureContext × UnitPreference, 400,740 checks in total.
    • Each displayed ± was compared with the unrounded engine bound95.
    • Results are quoted under R1.
  • Unit tests at HEAD, all green: engine 163, ar 40, data 14, app 43. :engine:koverVerify passes.
  • Release APK at HEAD (app/build/outputs/apk/release/app-release.apk, R8 full mode):
    • I inspected the R8 mapping, the merged release manifest and the dex (dexdump).
  • I took and released the build lock for every Gradle run.

2. Honesty contract, end to end

Check Result
Shown ± ≥ the engine's 95% bound after rounding Not strictly. 178 of 400,740 displays (0.04%) are short by ≤ 0.0086 mm. For example, "74 in ± 1.7" against an engine bound of 1.70034 in. Cause: MeasurementComposer.rounded() rounds σ to the nearest 0.01 mm before Units rounds up. Units alone gives 0 violations. The size is negligible, but the invariant is the product's #1 promise and the fix is one line. → R1
Badge matches Yes. The badge comes from the same (rounded) Measurement as the ±. The rectangle badge is forced to ROUGH on inconsistent geometry. The History badge is the stored quality of the saved reading.
Calibration only tightens when valid Yes. Calibrator accepts only if the effective scale σ is < 0.7 × the prior. The floor stays in every calibrated length. Points off the plane, out of range, from another session or stale use the uncalibrated model. Tests cover floor→table, floor→wall, the 1 m span (TOO_NOISY) and two surfaces (NOT_ONE_SURFACE).
Nothing shown as "calibrated" when it isn't No. During measuring, the chip, the result-card date and the History flag are correct. But Home (home_calibrated_on) and Settings (settings_calibration_on, settings_clear_calibration_detail "Stop using the saved scale correction") present a calibration that by design is never applied once Measure is left. → R3
Calibration cannot be made circular No. KnownLengthCard hides the AR value after both ends are placed. But LiveReadout (MeasureHud.kt:106-113,293-303) shows the live AR distance with its ± while the user aims at the second end. A user who types that number gets k ≈ 1 accepted with a 0.3%-floor scale σ: a tighter ± with no correction behind it. TESTING.md §11 says this value is hidden. → R2
No reading without the tier floor Yes. The probe found 0 floor violations across live, committed, calibrated, rectangle and height readings. Freeze-frame readings go through the same composer. The only number shown without a ± is the coarse camera→target distance pill. It is labelled as such, but in imperial it shows whole inches (M3-5).

3. M2 gate criteria (PLAN §4, §6)

Criterion Status
First installable build Met. builds/SpaceView-M2-fixed-debug.apk (37 MB debug) is byte-identical to app/build/outputs/apk/debug/app-debug.apk at HEAD. A debug-signed R8 release APK is also at app/build/outputs/apk/release/app-release.apk (19.5 MB universal). Rebuild both after R1–R3.
Distance / height / rectangle Met (MeasureMode, MeasurementComposer, mode selector, result cards).
Calibration Met, via known length (orchestrator decision in HANDOFF): 1–5 m tape span on one surface, card and A4 behind CALIBRATION_SMALL_REFERENCES_ENABLED = false. This deviates from PLAN §1 ("bank card / A4"). It is justified: the 1 cm floor makes small references always TOO_NOISY. Keep it documented until the bench test.
History Met. Room v1, newest first, delete, reformat on unit change, guarded I/O, saves exactly the reading shown.
Size ≤ 15 MB arm64 download Met (estimate). bundletool was not run locally. Deflate-9 of the release APK without armeabi-v7a gives 9.94 MiB: dex 3.65, native 3.14, assets 3.07, which includes about 2.5 MB of unused SceneView materials and skybox for M6. Play's brotli download should be at or below this. CI's bundletool gate (18 MiB) is the authoritative check once a remote exists.
No ads on AR screens Met. Banner only on History. Interstitials are blocked while any AR destination (Measure, Calibrate) is composed, and only follow a save.
CAMERA-first permissions Met. CAMERA is the only dangerous permission. It is requested from ArGate after an in-context explanation, with an Open settings path once blocked. Onboarding asks for nothing. Merged release manifest: CAMERA, INTERNET, ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE, AD_ID, three ACCESS_ADSERVICES_*. This matches ci/allowed-permissions.txt. The Data safety form must declare the advertising ID (M5/M6).

4. First-launch crash review (release / R8)

Nothing blocking was found.

  • AdMob APPLICATION_ID: the placeholder resolves in both build types. Debug and an untagged release use the Google test app ID (checked in the merged release manifest). Tag builds fail without real IDs.
  • Startup: Application.onCreate is empty and AppContainer is lazy. The onboarding flag is read through DataStore asynchronously. UMP and MobileAds.initialize run after the first frame, with initialize on Dispatchers.IO. No main-thread disk I/O.
  • R8: SpaceViewDatabase_Impl is kept under its name (Room reflection). The DataStore protobuf GeneratedMessageLite field-keep rule is in the R8 configuration. com.google.ar.core.* and com.google.android.filament.* are kept, via SceneView's blanket rules and ARCore's own. MainActivity and SpaceViewApplication are kept. ViewModels use initializer lambdas and routes are strings, so neither uses reflection.
  • Manifest: AR Required (camera.ar required, com.google.ar.core=required), configChanges covers rotation, locale and font scale, exported is set on the launcher.
  • AR availability: uses checkAvailabilityAsync, which never blocks the main thread. Install exceptions are mapped, not thrown.
  • Residual risk (device only): the debug and release APKs have not run on a phone yet. TESTING §13 (release flow) and the R4 additions are the real proof.

5. Required fixes (conditions of this PASS)

# Where Fix Check
R1 ar/src/main/kotlin/com/spaceview/ar/MeasurementComposer.kt:313-317 (Measurement.rounded()) Round σ up, not to nearest: val s = if (sigma > 0) kotlin.math.ceil(sigma / 1e-5 - 1e-9).coerceAtLeast(1.0) * 1e-5 else 0.0; Measurement(round(value, 1e-4), s). Add an ar unit test, as in the deleted probe: random composer readings × all contexts and preferences → parsed displayed ± ≥ unrounded engine bound95. Expect 0 violations.
R2 app/src/main/kotlin/com/spaceview/app/measure/MeasureHud.kt:106-113 (and LiveReadout :287-303) While calibration is CalibrationStep.Measuring && calibration.reference == null, do not show the live value: pass a flag to LiveReadout that skips the DimensionLabel, but keep the target-distance pill. Update TESTING §11 ("The measured AR value is not shown before you type") so it holds during aiming too.
R3 app/src/main/res/values/strings.xml:19,201,202,204,206 and the same keys in values-hi/strings.xml:19,201,202,204,206; HomeScreen.kt:146-152; SettingsScreen.kt:191-197 Stop presenting a saved calibration as active. For example: home_calibrated_on → "Last calibrated %1$s. It applies only while that Measure screen stays open." (or show the default detail on Home); settings_calibration_on → "Last calibrated %1$s (only used in the session it was made in)"; settings_clear_calibration_detail / _body → "Delete the saved calibration record".
